# Claktory manual

Claktory is where a person's agents live and work: persistent agents, each with its own name, prompt, model and folder, on the person's own machine or server. This manual covers what Claktory can do and how, in the app and as an agent.

## What's in the app

<!-- admin: app-claktories | The rail of Claktories at the window's left: several connected at once, switching, adding, naming, coloring and removing one; the phone's list. -->
### Claktories
- The app can be connected to several Claktories at once: this Mac's, and others on other machines (a Linux server, another Mac), each paired with this device. All of them stay connected and live; nothing is shared or merged between them, and the window shows one at a time.
- Each Claktory has a name and a color of its own, the same on every device that connects to it: its name starts as its computer's name (a Mac's Computer Name, from System Settings → General → Sharing, or the host name elsewhere), and its color is picked for it, different from one Claktory to the next.
- A thin rail at the window's far left has a tile for each, on a wash of its color: this Mac's (a laptop glyph) first, then the others in the order they were added, each with its name's initials ("mac-mini" is "MM") in its color. The one showing is tinted more, with an orange mark at the window's edge. Click another to switch the whole window to it at once, as it was left. Hovering one shows its name, whether it's connected and how (directly, or by an indirect route that may be a little slower), and since when.
- At a glance: a tile waves softly while one of its agents works, has at its corner how many things there wait for your answer (an orange count) or, otherwise, a dot while one has something new, a pulsing orange dot while it's connecting or reconnecting, and a red dot when it can't connect or doesn't allow this device. A Claktory that isn't connected yet is faint.
- **+** at the bottom of the rail opens **Add a Claktory**: the Claktories on {{user}}'s account that aren't here yet, under "On your account · <email>", each a row that asks "Connect to <name>?" in place, with Connect (signed out, it asks for the email first); and **Use a code instead**, where the pairing code made for this device on that Claktory (Settings → Devices → Add a device…) is pasted or scanned, checked in the same dialog, and connected. When every one on the account is here already, it says so, with **Use a code**. On a Mac without its own Claktory, **Set up Claktory on this Mac** is at its foot. The Claktories already here are only in the rail, to switch to and remove.
- Right-click a tile to change its name or color (for every device; an empty name goes back to its computer's name; only while it's connected) or, for one on another machine, **Remove…** it: this device disconnects, forgets its code and leaves that Claktory's devices. Its agents and work stay there. Recordings not sent to it yet are deleted from this device too, and the question says how many when there are any. When this is the only device connected to it, the question says so and how to connect again: `factoryd pair` in a terminal there or, when it's on {{user}}'s account, **On your account** in Add a Claktory.
- On iPhone, the conversation fills the screen and the **Menu** button at its top left (or a swipe from the screen's left edge) opens a drawer: the Claktories down its left side, then the one showing, with its name, its connection in a word ("Connected", "Reconnecting…", "Can't connect"; tap it for more and **Reconnect**), a search field that narrows the list to the agents and pipes whose name, role or project matches, **Needs you** with how many things wait (tap it for everything waiting on one page, to answer there, as on a Mac), and its projects, agents and pipes (a report's **›** at its row's end shows or hides the agents under it), with Map, Usage, Marketplace, Settings and What's new at the end. Tap another Claktory to see its agents (the drawer stays open), and an agent or pipe to open its conversation; swipe the drawer back, or tap the dimmed conversation, to close it. Each Claktory's tile shows its news and connection; a long press on one changes its name or color, or removes it, and **+** pairs another by scanning or pasting its code. At the largest text sizes a **Claktories** row opens the list of them instead, and over a first-launch step "‹ Claktories" at the top goes to that list.
- An iPhone, iPad or Android device left untouched for a minute, with nothing arriving, checks its connection every 25 seconds instead of every few, so its radio can rest. A connection that drops silently meanwhile can still show "Connected" for up to about a minute; the next touch, or coming back to Claktory, checks it at once.
- On iPhone, buttons, fields, switches and dropdowns are sized for a finger (at least 44 points, their text at iOS's 17 points, growing with the system's text size), a dropdown's choices open in a sheet, text in a field selects the iPhone's way (handles, magnifier, the system's Copy and Paste menu), and what a Mac shows in a dialog rises from the bottom as a sheet: drag it down, or tap above it, to close it.
- On Android, Back (the button or the gesture) closes what's on top first: the keyboard, then a dialog or sheet, then a page (Settings, Usage…) or the drawer, and at the conversation it leaves the app. A tablet or a wide window works the same: a dialog first, then a pane expanded over the window (as its **Back to …** does) or a page shown in place of the conversation, then the panes beside the conversation, one at a time, the one opened last first. From **Add a Claktory**, Back goes to the list of Claktories, then to the Claktory that was showing. Resizing the window never closes a dialog; only switching to another Claktory does. Narrow Android tablet windows use the phone layout, so Settings opens as a page instead of squeezing the message box.

- On Android, going to another app starts a 25-second connection grace period. Claktory lets messages, voice messages and uploads already on their way continue while Android allows, even beyond that grace period, then disconnects to save data and battery. A website shared over a pipe that you opened from Claktory counts as use while its tab is on screen: the connection stays while Android lets it, nothing promised beyond that. Android can stop the connection sooner; interrupted sends resume when you return. Returning also brings the sidebar and conversation up to date. Android does not get notifications yet.

<!-- admin: app-sidebar | The sidebar: agents and projects, folders, the working wave, unseen marks, dragging and nesting, the footer, provider usage circles. -->
### Sidebar
- Drag the sidebar's right edge to make it narrower or a little wider (200 to 300 points; 248 by default); double-click the edge to go back to the default. The width is kept in Claktory, so it's the same on every device.
- At the top, on their own, are the agents that aren't in any project (the workspace's agents), such as Rivet.
- **Projects** lists projects (folder icon), each with its agents underneath. New agents always go in a project. A project's agents form one tree, each under the agent it reports to, whatever folder it works in. A project can have **folders** (such as "App" for its code and "Research" for write-ups): an agent's folder shows on its row after its role ("Researcher · Ops", with a folder glyph) when it isn't its lead's folder (at the top level, the project's own); it's changed in the agent's settings, never by a drag. Click or tap a project's row to fold its pipes and agents away or show them again (folded, it shows what waits in it, a dot for something new and the wave while one works); the fold is the same on every device. Projects move the same way: drag one above or below another (on a phone, touch and hold it first; let go without moving and it's the project's menu, as before), such as to keep the most used at the top; the order is the same on every device, and an archived project keeps its place.
- New projects and agents are set up by Rivet, which picks the prompt, model and extensions that suit what {{user}} wants. The small "+" by **Projects**, and the one on a project's row while the pointer is on it (or on the focused project's name), open Rivet's conversation with the request started in its message box, "Set up a new project for " or "Add an agent to <project> that ", for {{user}} to finish and send; anything already typed to Rivet stays, with the request on a new line after it. Hovering the "+" says what it adds ("New project", "New agent in Bakery"). When there's no Rivet, it has no model available, or it's paused on a usage limit, the "+" (and **Add an agent…**) opens the New project or New agent form instead; a paused Rivet's form says so at its top ("Rivet is paused until your Claude limit resets. Set it up here instead."), with **Continue on** another provider when Rivet's wait offers one, which then hands it to Rivet after all. With no agents yet, the window says "Choose Add an agent… in a project's ⋯ menu." New project shows, under the name as it's typed, the folder the project will get ("Folder: ~/.factory/workspace/bakery"; "Folder on Mac Studio: …" from another device).
- Each agent shows as its character: a small, living shape in the agent's color (circle, square, triangle, diamond, pentagon or hexagon) with the same two eyes as every agent. Claktory picks the shape from the agent's id unless one is chosen in its settings, so renaming or moving it never changes it; Rivet starts as a blue hexagon. Its eyes show its mood: idle, glancing about and blinking now and then; thinking, looking up; running a tool, looking down at the work; something new to see, pleased; its last run failed, eyes down; no model, half-lidded; asleep after 15 minutes idle, eyes closed. The same character, smaller and resting, marks the agent wherever it's named: beside its messages, as an owner, in pickers. With reduced motion every face holds still in its mood. While the agent works, a soft wave of its color sweeps across its row, again and again, until the run ends (with reduced motion, the row is tinted in its color instead); a thin light runs along its header's colored line too.
- An agent's row shows its name and, when it has one, its **role** under it in smaller, fainter type ("Mike" over "Coordinator"); a row with a role is a little taller. The role shows the same way in the agent's header and in the Messaging panel, and faintly after the name over its messages and in pickers. Set it in the agent's settings, or when creating it with the New agent form.
- When an agent finishes (or fails) while you're looking elsewhere, its name turns bold and bright, with a dot in its color at the end of the row: something happened. Opening the agent clears it. Stopping an agent yourself doesn't mark it.
- When something in an agent's conversation waits for {{user}}'s answer (a question it can't go on without, a secret it asked for, a service it proposes to connect, a message it proposes for Rivet, a secret it wants to use after reading a pipe), its row ends with an orange badge saying how many, in place of the dot, until {{user}} answers. Clicking the agent opens its conversation at the oldest one when it's news (among the latest messages, with nothing from {{user}} after it); otherwise the conversation opens at its end as usual, and clicking the badge itself goes to the oldest one, however far back. A collapsed agent, a folded project and, in a focused project, "‹ All projects" show the badge for what they hide.
- **Needs you**, at the top of the sidebar while anything waits for {{user}}'s answer, gathers it all in one place with how many: every agent's and chat's waiting requests, every pipe's approval, held messages and loop pause, a provider's sign-in that expired, agents and chats waiting on a provider's usage limit or credit (one item per provider, saying who waits and until when, with **Try now**, a usage reset the provider's accounts have, and **Continue on OpenRouter** or another provider that can carry them, for all of them at once after asking; **Sign in…** when no other provider is signed in), secrets agents are waiting for while they're locked, a recovery key not yet saved, and a move of this Claktory an agent started, opening to the same confirm that floats over the window. Click it to open focus mode (below) on a computer or a full-width iPad, or the Needs you page in a narrow iPad window (on iPhone, from the drawer). Each item says who asks and what, and how long it has waited (standing out past a day), newest first, after what stops agents. Each is one request, answerable there: a question, a request for a secret or a proposal about a checklist item shows the item above it ("On its checklist: …", or another agent's) with **Show in checklist**, and an item with two such requests waiting is two entries. A checklist item waiting on {{user}} with no request about it (never asked, dismissed, or to be asked again later) isn't in Needs you: it's the agent's to settle, and shows only on its checklist. **Sign in to <provider> again** starts that sign-in and shows it in Settings → Providers, saying whether agents use {{user}}'s other accounts meanwhile or wait; **Unlock the secrets on <name> with its recovery key** opens the box for it. **Save the recovery key for <name>** (named, since each Claktory has its own key, even on one account) shows the key when asked, with **Copy** and **Download** (a text file named "Claktory recovery key (<name>).txt" saying whose key it is and the day, saved where {{user}} chooses: the save dialog on a Mac or Android, Files on an iPhone or iPad, never the share sheet; Settings → Account's **Show recovery key…** has both too), then asks {{user}} to type back one block of it, a different one each time; once it matches, the item goes on every device, until a new key replaces it. Click any other item to open the request itself and answer it right there, with the same controls as in the conversation, or **Open conversation** (**Open chat**, **Open pipe**) to go to it. A question asking {{user}} to review a document names the file, with **Review**: it opens the file beside the asking agent's conversation, filling the window, with the question at its foot: **Approve** or **Dismiss**, or type a comment and choose **Submit** (**Send** when the agent asked for a written answer) or press Return; answered, the file closes and {{user}} is back where they pressed Review. What {{user}} answers stays on the page, marked Answered, until they leave it. A request its agent changed while it waited (`ask_update`, `proposal_update`) shows its new version in place, wherever it is, with "Updated · 14:32" (Updated in the signal color) and the agent's reason in its words, and Needs you lists it from that time, "Updated 2 min ago", among the newest; what {{user}} was typing stays, and an answer they sent to the version before is set aside, never lost: the request says "June changed this after your answer." with what they said ("You said: “…”"), and their words stay to send again, so they never answer something they didn't see. A request {{user}} answered with words (a reply, or a comment on Approve or Dismiss) can become a short conversation: when its agent answers back on it (`ask_reply`), the request comes back to Needs you, at the top, with the talk so far above its buttons, oldest first, and beneath them **Reply** to keep talking and **Resolve**, which ends it with no decision ("Resolved", in History too); its usual buttons still decide it, and a spoken answer works as before. On a Mac, a chip at the top right of every conversation ("2 need you") says the same while anything waits; click it to open focus mode at that conversation's request. A proposal to Rivet has **Dismiss** on its item, folded, so a stale one goes in one click. **History**, beside **Waiting** at the page's top, lists what {{user}} answered, approved, declined or dismissed in the last 30 days (pipe messages they sent, let in or discarded, loop pauses they continued, app access Rivet proposed, each model or effort change an agent made with `models_set`, and each change Rivet or {{user}} made to what an app reads and which agents use it, with Rivet's reason as Rivet's words and **Undo**, which puts back what the change replaced (anything changed since stays as it is, and it says so), and each time Rivet put an agent on another model or effort, since that changes what {{user}} spends, with Rivet's reason and **Undo**, which puts back the model and effort it replaced unless they changed since (a click opens Rivet's conversation at the change, or that agent's settings), too; commands they ran or dismissed in a request, under Claktory's heading ("Run this command on" and the computer's name) with the command and how it went, never what it printed; questions and proposals their agents withdrew, shown as withdrawn with the agent's reason as its own words; secrets agents gave up on their own ("Mista gave up Cloudflare"), with the agent's reason; other access agents gave up on their own (a pipe, or only reading or leading one; an app; messaging an agent of another project: "Mista left the pipe "Launch"", "Mista gave up Charts"), with the agent's reason; changes Rivet made with no request of their own because {{user}} approved them in an agent's proposal, with "Approved in Bruno's proposal", which opens that proposal; secrets agents saved from a command's output (`save_as`), with the secret's description in the agent's words; not questions that clearing a conversation closed unseen), newest first: who asked, what, how it ended in the request's own words ("Couldn't do step 2 of 4", "Stopped at step 2 of 4", "Gave Hetzner", "Left to Rivet"; with their answer, and "Updated twice" when its agent changed it meanwhile) and when; its search field finds requests by agent or words, **Show earlier** goes further back, and a click opens the conversation (or chat, or pipe) at the request, or, for {{user}}'s own app change, the app in Marketplace → Apps.
- **Focus mode** (Needs you in the sidebar or ⌘⇧N on a computer or a full-width iPad; in a narrow iPad window, **Focus mode** at the end of the Needs you page's Waiting tab; Done or Esc to leave) turns the whole window into what waits for {{user}}'s answer, one request at a time: the queue at the left (what stops agents first, then the newest; a new request goes in at the top of its kind without taking the one shown, and the next after an answer is the row below; **Done** at its top, then **Waiting** and **History**, what {{user}} answered, each shown in the middle as it ended; ⌘F finds requests by agent or words; the other Claktories' counts under it, a click carrying on there), the request in the middle with its own buttons, and its conversation at the right (**Context**, C to hide it, with the agent's message box for anything that isn't an answer), followed without selecting the agent. ⌘↩ is the request's yes (Submit with words typed; held, it records what {{user}} says into the request's field), ⌘⌫ its no, offering five quick reasons (1–5) and ⌘⌫ again for none; J/K or ↓/↑ move between requests, R goes into the field, where Return sends and Shift+Return starts a line, G goes to the conversation. With nothing left it says so, naming any other Claktory where something still waits, with **Done**. On a Mac, View → **Focus on Needs You** (⌘⇧N) turns it on and off, and a notification about a device waiting to be allowed, an alert or what waited out quiet hours opens it there. Once Claktory accepts an answer the next request comes up with a receipt ("Approved · June"); a request answered on another device stays, saying where, until {{user}} moves on, and a request its agent just changed takes no yes or no for a second. Whether a device is in focus mode is kept by Claktory, for each device, while the app runs: the app always opens out of it. On an iPhone, and an iPad in Slide Over or a narrow Split View, Needs you opens on its list, so {{user}} picks what to answer: a request tapped opens alone on its own screen, its answer pinned above the keyboard, and goes back to the list once Claktory accepts the answer (one with something to show once answered, such as a pipe invite's code, stays until Back). A phone has no focus mode: its list is the way through. On such an iPad, **Focus mode** goes through them one request a screen ("3 of 7" opens the queue, ˄ ˅ move between requests), and Back, or Done once nothing is left, returns to the list. On every device Needs you's list holds still under the finger: a request coming in above goes in without moving what shows, a pill at the top ("↑ 1 new", "↑ 1 urgent") scrolling up to it, and one answered elsewhere fades where it was, the rest closing up once no finger is down. On an iPad (⌘⇧N with a keyboard) it fits the window: the queue, the request and Context side by side where there's room, Context folding away as the window narrows, and one request a screen in Slide Over or a narrow Split View; a PDF or picture a request asks to have reviewed shows on the stage to draw on with Apple Pencil, its review bar the request's answer, and the drawing goes with it.
- An agent's question is a request in its conversation: what it needs and why, and either a box for {{user}}'s answer with **Answer**, or **Approve** when it only needs a yes (a comment typed there turns it into **Submit**, below). **Dismiss** leaves it to the agent's judgment, with what's typed in the answer box as {{user}}'s comment. Its text can be selected and copied, and a command in it has **Copy** beside it.
- Every request where {{user}} approves or dismisses something an agent hears the outcome of (a question, a secret asked for or a use of one, a proposal, a service to connect, a pipe to approve, a held pipe message, a request for starting a pipe, a suggested change) has its field open from the start, above its buttons ("Reply to Mike"), up to 2,000 characters; it never takes the keys by itself. Every request's row reads the same way: a line at the left saying what pressing does, then its no, any second action, and its one filled button last (on a phone, the thumb row: the no at the left and the filled button at the right, under the line). A request's text longer than 8 lines folds its middle, keeping its first lines and its last paragraph, with **Show all**; Find opens it where it matches. While the field is empty the request shows its usual buttons; once anything is typed in a plain yes or no request they give way to one **Submit**, the line saying "Submit sends your words instead of approving." (Return in the field is Submit too, and never approves; on a phone's on-screen keyboard return is a new line): the comment goes as a reply, neither a yes nor a no, and the agent reads what {{user}} wants from their words. A question is told "{{User}} replied to your question …" and counts as answered (an item it's about isn't asked again); a proposal reaches Rivet with the comment as what to do. In a request that carries something out exactly as shown (a service to connect, Rivet's app access, a pipe to approve, a held pipe message, a use of a secret), Submit carries out nothing: the request closes as Replied (a pipe keeps waiting), the agent gets the comment, and comes back with a new request if it still fits; a held message isn't sent and goes back to its writer. On a suggested change, Submit is a reply on its thread, and the suggestion stays open. A request whose yes carries something {{user}} made (a secret's values, a key to generate, files to import, a step done) keeps its buttons as they are, and the words go with whichever they press, Dismiss included; a step adds **Send** (see Ask); a written answer's words are its answer; starting a pipe and an **Execute** request take words only with Dismiss, as their field says before anything is typed. Return in the field sends only where words alone answer (Submit, Send, a written answer) and does nothing on the others. Words typed in a request whose agent takes it back or changes it stay: changed, the field says "Mike updated this request while you were typing"; taken back, the request shows them as "Not sent", with **Copy** and **Send to Mike**, which puts them in Mike's conversation. To approve or dismiss with nothing more, leave the field empty. Dismissing a question, a request for a secret, a proposal or a held pipe message tells its agent quietly, with whatever next wakes it, rather than waking it: often it was settled elsewhere, in the agent's chat say. While a decision goes, the request's line says "Sending…"; refused, it says why, and everything stays as it was. A decision or answer in any request or review (with its comment) cut off by the connection says "Sends when you're back online." and goes by itself once the connection is back, what's typed staying in its field meanwhile; Claktory takes it once. On a pipe it goes only to this side's agent (the message's writer, the pipe's lead, or the agent whose request started the pipe, else Rivet), never to the other side.
- Drag an agent onto a project to move it there, at the top level, or onto Rivet's row to move it out of any project, reporting to Rivet (for no lead, change Project in its settings instead, which says under the field what that changes: "June will work in Bakery's folder and can message only agents in Bakery."). Dropped between the rows under Rivet, it's pinned instead and stays in its project. Its conversation moves with it, and so do its reports. Claktory asks first ("Move Speedwagon to Bakery?"), listing what changes: the agents that come along, the folder it will work in and that its files stay where they are, whom it can message, whom it will report to, which pipes it stops answering and who takes over, and that a working agent carries on from its next step. **Move** moves it, and its row then says "Now in Bakery · Undo" for 8 seconds.
- Pin an agent to keep it under Rivet: Pin in its row's menu (right click, or touch and hold) or its ⋯ menu. Drag pins to reorder them, or use Move up and Move down in a pin's menu. On a Mac, or an iPad with a keyboard, ⌘1 to ⌘9 open the pins' conversations in order. On an iPad the row's menu opens beside it. A pinned agent stays in its project too. Rivet can pin one for you. Pins are the same on every device; deleting an agent, moving it out of projects or archiving its project unpins it.
- The sidebar shows who reports to whom: an agent's reports (the agents it leads in its project) sit indented under it; an agent that reports to an admin agent (such as Rivet) stays at its project's top level. Arrange agents by dragging: drop one on the middle of another agent's row (the row is outlined) to make it that agent's report; drop it on the upper or lower edge of a row (a line shows where) to put it there: among its own level, that only changes the order; at another level, it reports to that level's lead (or to no one, at the top). While a drop would change its lead, the dragged name says so, with the agents that come along ("Reports to Mike · its 3 agents come too", "Reports to no one"). A drop never changes the folder an agent works in. A drop into a collapsed project or agent opens it, and the list scrolls to the moved row if it's out of sight. After a drop that changes its lead, its row says so for 8 seconds in place of its role, "Now reports to Mike · **Undo**": Undo puts it and its reports back where they were, leads and order too; on a Mac ⌘Z does it while the line shows (not while typing in a field), by touch Undo is a button at the row's end and the line wraps rather than being cut, and VoiceOver offers Undo as an action on the row. On iPhone, hold an agent still a moment in the drawer, then drag; closing the drawer before the 8 seconds are up moves Undo to the foot of the screen for the rest of them. If Claktory refuses a lead (one that would make a loop, say), it says why. The arrow beside an agent with reports collapses or expands them. A collapsed agent shows the wave while one of its hidden agents works, and shows the unseen marker when one has something new. The order, leads and collapsing are kept by Claktory, the same on every device, and a lead set in an agent's settings or by Rivet shows here too.
- Every project row has a ⋯ (on a Mac while the pointer is on the row; always by touch), and a right click (or a touch and hold let go in place) opens the same menu: **Files**, **Add an agent…**, **Connect a pipe…**, **New pipe group…** (once it has a pipe), **Show only this project** (**Show all projects** once focused; see below; not on a phone) and **Project settings…**. From the keyboard, ← folds a project, → unfolds it, and Return, Shift-F10 or the menu key open its menu. While a project's Files is open, its row shows a Files glyph that closes it. **Project settings…** opens its settings, which start with its **Folder**: the path (with **Show in Finder** on the Mac that runs the agents, as each of its folders has). Rename it, archive it (**Archive project**, at once: it and its agents leave the sidebar and the rail's marks, and nothing is deleted; with pipes, its note says they stay open but nothing answers on them until it's unarchived), delete it (**Delete project…** asks first, saying its agents and their conversations go, its folder and files stay, and which of its pipes close, their messages here deleted for good; **Archive instead** is beside it), and keep its folders (rename one, point it at another path, remove it, or "Add a folder…", which asks Rivet to set it up, or opens a short form when Rivet can't run). Removing a folder moves its agents to the project's folder; no files are deleted.
- Archived projects are listed under **Archived (N)** at the bottom of the project list: click it to open the list, then **Unarchive** on one to bring it back (or click it for its settings). An archived project's agent opened from a link says so above its message box, with **Unarchive**.
- **Projects** and **Chats** are the sidebar's two sections, side by side in importance: both headings always show, however many projects or chats there are. Click a heading to fold that section away or open it again; folded, it shows how many it holds and whether anything in it waits for {{user}}. Which are folded is kept for every device.
- **Chats**, under the projects, lists {{user}}'s 20 most recent chats (a speech bubble and the title; bold with a dot when it replied while elsewhere, an orange badge while a proposal to Rivet waits), then **Show all** for every chat, with a search by title (on iPhone, in the drawer, with **See all**). Its "+" (or **New chat** while there are none) opens an empty chat at once; one left without a message is dropped. A chat opens in place of an agent's conversation: a plain bar with its title and model, the conversation, and a box for text only (no files or voice yet). Esc or **Stop** stops a reply. Click the title to rename it (left empty, Claktory names it again); its **…**, a right click on its row or touching and holding it on iPhone offer **Rename…**, **Change model…** (the models to choose from, with a search; it applies from the chat's next reply, even mid-reply) and **Delete…**, which asks first and can't be undone. A chat whose model can't run offers **Change model…** beside Sign in… in place of its box. A proposal to Rivet in a chat is approved or dismissed in its request, or from Needs you.
- At the bottom, **Settings** opens Settings (⌘, too, on a Mac) and **More** opens a menu of the rest: **Usage** (tokens by agent, provider and model; see Usage), **Monitors** (every agent's scheduled jobs, reminders and watches; see the Monitors extension), the **Map**, the **Marketplace**, and **What's new**, with a check by the one showing; More glows while one of them is open. Your avatar at the end of the row opens Settings, where your profile is (see Settings). What's new is every version of Claktory, newest first, on a timeline, each with its date and what it added, changed and fixed; the one running is marked "This version", and any not installed yet "Update to get it". On a Mac each opens as a pane right of the selected agent's conversation (see Panes), so the conversation stays readable beside it; its button shows it's open, and clicking it again closes the pane. One already open glows instead of opening twice. With no agent selected, or a pipe or chat showing, it takes the whole view, with Done (or Esc) to go back; on an iPhone it's a page over the conversation, with Back (or a swipe from the left edge) to go back. The footer also shows a sign-in in progress, and "Settings file has an error" when the settings file is broken. Clicking that offers "Restore last working settings", and **Tell Rivet**. Above the version, always, a line with a dot says how the app is connected to the Claktory it shows: "Running on this Mac" or "Connected to mac-mini" (a faint dot), "Reconnecting…" or "Connecting to mac-mini…" (a pulsing orange dot), "Can't connect to mac-mini" (red). Hovering it (or touching and holding it) says how it connects (directly, or by an indirect route that may be a little slower) and since when; clicking it opens the same with **Reconnect**, which connects afresh at once, the reason when it can't connect, and **Switch Claktory…**. Claktory's version is shown under it in small type; it's only text: the rail at the window's left (on an iPhone, the list of Claktories) switches and adds them. When a Claktory doesn't accept this device (it was removed, or its code was used by another device), the line says "<machine> doesn't allow this device" and stops trying; clicking it lists the Claktories this device knows: **This Mac**, and each one on another machine it has connected to (hover one to Forget it); click one to show it. **Connect to another Claktory** there takes the pairing code made for this device on that Claktory (see Settings → Devices) and connects.
- Wherever Claktory shows an error (a broken settings file, a run that failed, an update that didn't work or was undone, a request it refused, a sign-in that failed, remote access, a folder Files can't read, a failed monitor), **Tell Rivet** beside it opens Rivet's conversation with the error typed in its message box, ready to send, so Rivet can look into it and fix it. It shows only while Rivet can run. While Rivet waits on a usage limit, its place says so instead ("Rivet is waiting for your Claude usage to reset at Fri 18:00"), with **Continue on OpenRouter** when another signed-in provider can run Rivet now (not while the settings file has an error, since switching changes it), or **Sign in…** when none is. A run that failed because the provider refused the agent's key or sign-in says so instead ("Anthropic refused Rivet's key.") with the way to fix it: **Change key…** or **Sign in again…** starts that sign-in in Settings → Providers, replacing the refused account (**Add a key…** for a key from the environment, which can't be replaced, adds one before it).
- Text you read can be selected anywhere: the conversation (tool calls' input and output too), Claktory's notes, the panels, Settings, What's new, the Marketplace, dialogs and errors. Buttons, the sidebar and other things you click aren't. A right click selects the word under it (or keeps what's selected) and opens a menu with **Copy**; ⌘C copies too. In a conversation the menu also has **Reply**, which quotes the message under the pointer into the message box, ready for {{user}}'s answer; on iPhone, touch and hold a message and choose **Reply**. A message from another agent is quoted with its sender's name ("Kenji wrote:"). Copied text keeps its shape: a blank line between paragraphs, each list item on its own line with its bullet or number, nested items indented under theirs, and a table's cells separated by tabs.
- Before those circles, "4/23" says how many agents are working out of all of them, in every project, with an orange dot while any are; hovering it (on iPhone, touching and holding it) names them, and clicking it opens the Map.
- Just above the cog and the bag, a small circle for each provider Claktory is signed in to (for a provider with several subscription accounts, one per account), in that provider's color with its product's logo inside (Claude's for Anthropic, OpenAI's for OpenAI and Codex, OpenRouter's). They wrap onto more rows as there are more. After them, always (with one provider, several or none), a **+** ("Add an AI provider") opens Settings → Providers to add another. For a subscription the ring around the logo fills as the weekly limit is used: empty at 0%, full at 100%. Hovering it shows the plan (such as "Claude Max 20x" or "ChatGPT Pro"; until it's known, how it's signed in), every limit's percentage used and when it resets, and when the numbers were last read; right after signing in it says the usage isn't read yet. If they couldn't be read, it says why and shows the last ones it had (the circle is faded); a dashed ring means there are no numbers yet. A provider signed in with an API key has no limits, so it shows just its logo, with no ring; an OpenRouter key with a spending limit set on it has a ring that fills as the limit is used, and its card says what's left and what it spent today ("$3.20 left of $10 a day · $0.80 today", or "$0.80 today · no spending limit on this key"), and why it can't run paid models when it can't (no credit yet, or its limit used up). In Settings → Providers, such a key's row says what's left beside it ("$12.40 left"). OpenRouter tells a key only about its own limit, never the account's balance. A subscription with usage resets left (Claude's 5-hour, weekly, full and Fable resets, ChatGPT's reset credits) lists them at the end of its card, each with how many are left and when they must be used by, and **Use…**: it asks first, then uses it and says how it went, with a link to the provider's own page when the provider refused Claktory. Nothing ever uses one on its own, and no agent can. A small dot on the circle means a limit one of its resets refills is 90% used or more. For a provider with several subscriptions, a dot under a circle marks the one Claktory uses now. An account shows as in use (in Settings → Providers too) only while an agent or chat runs on a model of that provider; one nobody runs on just shows it's signed in, and its provider's card in Settings → Providers says no agents or chats use its models yet. Claude's Fable models draw on a weekly limit of their own: an account at Fable's limit says so ("Fable: limit reached, back at Fri 18:00") apart from its other limits, and the account Fable agents use now is marked too, as in use for Fable. The card stays open while the pointer is on it; on iPhone, touch and hold the circle to open it.

<!-- admin: app-focused-project | Focusing a project, and its Files pane: folders, checkouts, .factoryignore and Show hidden files. -->
### A focused project, and its files
- **Show only this project**, in a project's ⋯ menu, focuses it: the sidebar shows only that project and its agents, with Rivet kept at the top so it's always a click away (it's the one who changes the project for you; it's there whenever there's an admin agent), and "‹ All projects" goes back to everything. Click (or right-click) the project's name at the top for the same menu as its row's. A new project opens focused. A phone (iPhone or Android) has no focus: its list always shows every project, each folding with a tap, even while a project is focused on a Mac or an iPad, and a project made there isn't focused.
- **Files:** **Files** in a project's ⋯ menu (by touch, also the file button on the focused project's name) opens the project's Files as a pane right of the selected agent's conversation when that agent is in the project, focused or not; while they're open, the project's row shows a Files glyph that closes them. When the agent shown is in another project (or no agent, a pipe or a chat is shown), they open as a page of their own instead, as Settings does, with the project's name and Done (or Esc), and select no agent: a file clicked there opens on the page in place of the list, and **‹ Files** (or Esc) goes back to the list where it was. At the top, Files show where the project's folder is (its whole path on hover), with **Show in Finder** on the Mac that runs the agents, where right-clicking a file offers **Show in Finder** too. On iPhone, tap a project's ⋯ in the drawer (or touch and hold it) and choose Files: they open as a page over the conversation, a file opens over them, and Back (or a swipe from the left edge) goes back. Files show the project's folder, the same one its agents work in, and update as they change files. Each of the project's folders follows as a section of its own, headed by its name and path; the project's own folder steps aside when it's empty and the project has folders. No directory shows twice: a folder that is the project's own folder, or inside it, shows only as its own section. After them, each agent here with its own checkout (the Own checkout extension) has a section too, "Builder's checkout", with its branch and path, showing the files as that agent has them. Click a folder to open or close it; click a file to open it in a viewer right of the Files (the next ones stack under it), or make its pane glow when it's open. Which folders are open, and Show hidden files, are kept per project, the same on every device. Files are a pane like any other: move, resize or close them.
- Files hide what the folder's `.factoryignore` lists (same syntax as `.gitignore`), such as `node_modules/` and build output. New projects get one with common entries; a folder without one hides those same entries. "Show hidden files" shows them too, dimmed.

<!-- admin: app-updates | The Update banner, what updating restarts, and going back when a new backend doesn't start. -->
### Updates
- Versions are numbered v0.1.0, v0.1.1, v0.1.2…, one up with every release; the middle number goes up (v0.2.0) when a new app and an older backend can't work together, so both update. Versions before v0.1.0 were named by date (2026.11.19-1958).
- An app and a Claktory whose middle numbers differ can't work together, so the app shows only which to update, over everything but the update banner and the rail of Claktories: "Update Claktory to use this app" when Claktory is older, "This app is too old for this Claktory" when the app is, each with both versions and how to update (Update in the banner when the latest release brings them together; otherwise on that Claktory's machine, or the iPhone app from where it was installed). What's new still opens over it.
- When a new version of Claktory is published, a banner at the top says so, with an Update button, and "What's new" to see what the update brings. It can't be dismissed. A Claktory installed with the online installer checks for new versions shortly after it starts and every half hour, and downloads one before offering it; nothing is installed until you click Update. A version taken back from the channel after it was downloaded is deleted and no longer offered (never the version running, or one built on this computer). An answer from the update server older than one Claktory already had is ignored, so an old one served again can't take an update back or offer an older one. It takes a new version only when Claktory's own release key signed it, and on a Mac only when Claktory's developer signed its app, so no one else can pass off an update as Claktory's, even if the place it's downloaded from were taken over; one that isn't signed so is never offered. (A channel in a folder, for trying a release out, is taken unsigned.) **Check for updates** in Settings, beside the version, checks now instead of waiting (online, if this Claktory was installed that way, and for releases on its own computer) and says what it found in place: up to date, a new version downloading or ready to install with Update, or why it couldn't check. Asked again within 10 seconds, it gives the same answer. On iPhone it checks for the Claktory it's connected to, and its Update updates that Claktory. On an iPhone or iPad, Update only ever updates the Claktory, so the banner shows only when the Claktory has something to install; when only the device's own app is behind, a quiet line in its place says a newer Claktory app is in the App Store or TestFlight, and Close puts it away until the app opens again or a newer one comes. When the new version starts a new middle number, the offer names the paired devices whose app won't connect after it until it's updated ("Pixel won't connect after this update until its app is updated."), as the Claktory knows their app versions; and an iPhone, iPad or Android phone whose own app couldn't follow it offers no Update, saying to update its app first, from the App Store or TestFlight (on Android, with Download for its download page).
- The Android app updates apart from Claktory: the phone's browser downloads it from https://claktory.com/android, and Claktory's Update never replaces it. When a newer Android app is available, the menu and Settings → General say so, with **Download**, which opens that page; Android's installer then asks before it updates the app, and nothing installs by itself. When the app is too old for its Claktory, the screen that says so ("Update this app, then open it again.") has **Download** too, which opens the same page ("Opening…"), even if the list of releases can't be read; if the page can't open, it says "Couldn't open the download page. Try again."
- Updating restarts the app, and Claktory's backend when the release changed it. Agents that were working pick up where they left off, told that Claktory restarted; messages waiting for them are kept. A release that only changed the app doesn't restart the backend at all.
- If a new backend doesn't start, Claktory goes back to the one before and the banner says what happened, with "Try again".
- On a Mac, the first update that finds Claktory's launch settings older than a new install's (`~/Library/LaunchAgents/dev.factory.factoryd.plist`; today: running as an interactive program, so macOS doesn't throttle it and what agents run) brings them up to date once, about a minute after it starts, and restarts the backend once more to apply them. If the updated settings don't load, the old ones are put back. What it did is in `{{home}}/bin/launch-agent.log`.
- On a Claktory on another machine, Update updates that Claktory first (it has the new version already, so nothing downloads for it), then brings that version's app over from it: the banner says how far it is ("Getting the app ready on mac-mini…", then how much has downloaded, then "Checking the new app…"). It downloads in pieces sized to the connection, so a slow one keeps working; while the connection goes by a relay rather than directly, gently (small pieces, about 256 KB a second, about 3 minutes for a Mac app), so the relay and everything else on the connection keep flowing, and at the connection's own pace again as soon as it's direct; and when the connection drops it says "Waiting for the connection to mac-mini…" and carries on by itself once it's back. What has downloaded is kept beside the app until the update is done, so "Try again", even after quitting the app, carries on from there. The app is replaced only once it checks out (its size and checksum, that it's Claktory, and its signature; a download that doesn't match is downloaded again, once); the app then quits and opens again as the new version. A version this app can't work with (a new middle number) is downloaded first, and that Claktory updates only once it has. If anything goes wrong, the banner says why, with "Try again".

<!-- admin: protected-folders | Folders in Documents, Desktop, Downloads, iCloud Drive or a volume: when macOS asks, what agents are told when refused, and the Needs you item. -->
### Folders macOS protects
- macOS keeps Documents, Desktop, Downloads, iCloud Drive and other volumes (`/Volumes/…`) private until {{user}} lets an app in (System Settings → Privacy & Security → Files and Folders). On a Mac, agents' commands come from Claktory Agents, so it's Claktory Agents macOS asks about.
- Adding a project's folder in one of them, or pointing a folder there, reads the place at once, so macOS asks while {{user}} is there. In a project's settings, a folder's path in one says so under it ("macOS asks before Claktory Agents can use your Documents folder.", or how to turn it on once refused), and on the Mac that runs the Claktory, **Save** (or **Add folder**) first shows a sheet, "Let your agents work in Documents": **Continue** saves, and macOS asks; **Not now** goes back to the form with nothing saved. Projects already in such places get the same sheet once at the app's next launch, and are read when {{user}} continues.
- When macOS refuses, an agent's file tools (always) and bash (when its output says so) tell it so and not to work around it, and Needs you shows one item for the place, "Your agents can't work in Documents", with **Open System Settings** and **OK** (on {{user}}'s other devices, which Mac's System Settings to open, and **OK**). OK keeps the place refused; it comes back only after the place was let in and then refused again. Each item that ends goes to History, "Allowed later" when a check found the place let in after OK, and a click there opens Permissions.
- Claktory can't ask macOS whether a place is allowed, so it keeps what it last found, shown on the Mac that runs it in Settings → This device → Permissions, a row for each place checked ("Documents · On"; "Off" with what it stops and **Open System Settings**). Coming back to the front, the app checks the refused places again, which asks nothing, so a place let in from System Settings shows On.

<!-- admin: developer-tools | Faster builds on a Mac: add Claktory Agents under Privacy & Security → Developer Tools, so macOS stops checking each new program agents build. -->
### Developer Tools (faster builds on a Mac)
- macOS checks every new program the first time it runs, and agents build and run many (each test binary a build makes): a fraction of a second each, which adds up to most of a minute on a large test run. A program macOS counts as a developer tool spares everything it starts these checks.
- What agents run is started by Claktory's supervisor, which on a Mac installed (or reinstalled) with this version's installer is an app of its own, **Claktory Agents**, in `{{home}}/bin/Claktory Agents.app`. Installs from before run a bare program there instead, which macOS can't list; reinstalling Claktory (its installer again, over the installed Claktory) brings the app. Updates never change the supervisor.
- To add it: in Terminal, `spctl developer-mode enable-terminal` (once, so System Settings shows the Developer Tools list); then System Settings → Privacy & Security → Developer Tools → **+**, press ⇧⌘G, enter `{{home}}/bin/Claktory Agents.app`, click Open, and make sure it's switched on.
- To check it: have an agent run `cd "$(mktemp -d)" && printf 'int main(void){return 0;}' > t.c && cc -o t t.c && time ./t`. Without the grant the first run of a new program takes about 0.1–0.2 s; with it, a few milliseconds.
- Until Claktory's releases are signed with its Developer ID, macOS knows Claktory Agents by its exact build, so after a reinstall it needs adding again (remove the old entry with **−** first).

<!-- admin: app-agent-view | An agent's view: header, settings form, message box and drafts, queued messages, slash commands, the conversation, events, back and forward. -->
### Agent view (click an agent)
- The header is tinted with the agent's color. On a Mac or iPad it shows the agent's character and name (with its role), which open the agent's settings; **Model · effort ▾**, which opens a small popover to choose the model and reasoning effort, each applied at once, with "Changed to … · Undo" for a few seconds after; **Checklist** with how many items are open and, while background tasks run, **Tasks · 2 running**, each opening or closing its panel beside the conversation (over it, from the right, when there's no room; Esc closes it); a meter of its context, tokens used out of the compaction threshold (hover it, or tap it on an iPad, for what that means); and **⋯** (More options): **Find…** (⌘F), **Other versions** when there are some, the agent's panels that have something to show, **Agent settings…** and **Extensions…**. In a narrow window they give way in order: Tasks' and Checklist's words, the meter, the effort, the role, then the name is cut. On iPhone the header shows Back or Menu, the character and name over "role · model", Needs you and ⋯ (Search, the open files, versions and the agent's extensions); tapping the name opens the agent's settings. The agent's settings, in this order: **Or ask Rivet to change <name>** at the top (it opens Rivet's conversation with "Change <name>: " in its message box, nothing sent; with changes in the form, it first asks **Save your changes to <name>?** with **Discard** and **Save**; not in Rivet's own settings), its model and effort side by side, **Fallback model** (what a step its model declines is tried again on; **Default** unless one is picked), name and role (optional; empty for none) side by side, its appearance (its character, its **Shape**, Automatic or one picked, and its color), its prompt (folded to six lines when longer, with **Show all**, and unfolded once clicked into; Rivet's says whether it's Claktory's default, and once changed, **Reset to default** puts the default back), project and its folder (when the project has folders: the project's own folder or one of them), its **Lead** (the agent it reports to, which sees how it's set up: one in its project, or an admin agent; None for none), its message box hint (what the empty message box says; left empty, "Message <name>"; several lines, one example per line, take turns, each sliding up as the next comes in, and hold still while the pointer is over the box), **Notify me**, its **Secrets** (a switch for each of the vault's secrets, on for those it has; the Secrets extension's box names them too), its extensions, folded to how many are on ("11 on · Change…"), and "Delete agent…". The New agent form asks in the same order: model and effort, name and role, color, prompt, project and folder. Under **Extensions**, once unfolded with **Change…**, those with something to show come first; a switch turns each extension on or off for this agent, and while it's on, its options show underneath: more switches, a menu of choices (such as how often the Checklist checks in), or a list of agents (click it to choose agents from other projects, grouped by project; agents in its own project are already included, shown checked and greyed). Changes apply with Save.
- If the agent's model can't run because its provider isn't signed in, the header says "No model available", with a "Sign in…" button that opens Settings at Providers.
- The message box: Enter sends, Shift+Enter adds a line. What's typed and not sent stays as that agent's draft, through switching agents, opening Settings or the Marketplace, and restarting or updating the app, on this device; sending (or a command that runs) clears it. A message shows in the conversation the moment it's sent, labeled "Sending…" until Claktory has it; one that can't be sent stays there with Retry and Edit to take it back to the box: "Not sent yet" when the connection dropped (it goes by itself once the connection is back), "Not sent" when Claktory refused it (point at it to see why). A message sent while the agent is working waits only until the agent's current step ends (a model reply, or a tool call such as a command finishing), then the agent reads it before going on. While it waits, it shows at the end of the conversation as its bubble, faded and labeled "Queued" (messages from other agents waiting for it show the same way, on the left); when the agent takes it, it turns into the normal bubble in place. The sidebar shows how many are waiting. Stop (or Esc) stops the run; what's queued stays, and the agent gets it right after, as a new run. A message from you also moves a long command the agent is running to the background (another agent's, once the command has run a minute), so it's read sooner.
- Files: drag files onto the window (Mac: the whole window dims and says whose message they'll go to, or why they can't go there, as in a chat, which takes text only, or with no conversation open; an iPad the same, from Files, Photos or another app, at every window size, each showing as Adding photo…, Adding video… or Adding file… until it's copied, and text dragged in from another app goes into the message box), paste them or a picture with ⌘V, or use the paperclip at the end of the message box (on iPhone and iPad: Take Photo, Choose from Photos, Choose from Files or Paste image; on Android: Take photo, Choose photos or videos, Choose files or Paste image. Take Photo, offered in the message box and in a request's answer where there's a camera, first says why Claktory asks for the camera, as its other permissions do, and opens the camera full screen; the photo, named for when it was taken, attaches as a chosen one does); they wait in the box as chips (× takes one out) and go with the next message, which may have no text. Sending uploads them to the agent's uploads folder, with progress on each, and the agent gets them with the message; a cut-off upload picks up where it stopped on Retry. In the conversation a picture shows as a thumbnail and any other file as a chip; a click opens it in a viewer beside the conversation. On Android, **Share** in another app (Photos, Files, Chrome…) offers Claktory for text, links, pictures and files: **Send to…** lists the agents and chats of every Claktory the phone is connected to, and choosing one opens its conversation with what was shared added to the message box, after anything already there, to check and send; nothing goes until {{user}} taps Send, and Cancel leaves the boxes as they were. A Claktory that's offline shows its connection state, not Loading…; its agents and chats appear when they arrive. A chat takes text only, so it can't be chosen for a share with files (its row says "Chats take text only"). A share takes up to 20 files, each up to 2 GB (more than 20 adds none, and says so), and text up to 100,000 characters (longer is cut, with a note).
- Voice messages (Mac): the mic at the end of the message box records one; the box shows a live waveform, the time and Cancel, and the mic becomes Send (Return sends too, Esc cancels). Recording stops by itself at 10 minutes and sends. A recording goes on while {{user}} goes elsewhere in the app (another conversation, Settings, Needs you, a page over the conversation, the drawer on a phone): a bar at the top says whom it's for, the time and the sound, with Cancel and Send (on a phone a strip across the top, the screen moved down under it so it hides nothing; elsewhere floating over the middle of the window's top); Send sends it to the conversation it was started in, through its outbox as from the box, and says so ("Sent to June"); the name goes back to that conversation, where the box shows the recording again; Cancel on one over 30 seconds asks first (Keep or Delete). On a phone the same holds for a tapped recording, while one held with a finger stays with the finger. There's one recording at a time: a mic elsewhere says which one goes on. The recording is kept on the device (in the app's private storage, never backed up) until Claktory has the message: on a weak connection the message waits, "Not sent yet", with Retry and Delete in place of Edit, and goes by itself once the connection is back, picking up the upload where it stopped; if the app quits or is closed first, it comes back to its conversation on the next launch and goes then. Claktory takes it once, however often it's sent. The first time on a Claktory without its speech model, it asks before downloading it (about 740 MB, on the computer Claktory runs on) and starts the download while you record. The message shows at once as a bubble with its length, labeled "Transcribing…" ("Downloading the speech model… 312 of 740 MB" while the model downloads); the computer Claktory runs on turns it into text, so the recording goes nowhere else, and the agent gets the words as your message, in order with what you send after it. Then the bubble shows what was heard, with ▶ and the length to play the recording. Text in the box goes with a recording as one message: recorded while the box has something written (or files), the recording waits in the box as a **voice note**, a chip with its length and × to take it out (asking first when it's over 30 seconds). While recording so, the box's Send reads Add (Add voice note on a phone, where the mic sits beside Send while there's text), and while a note waits the mic steps aside: one to a message. Send sends it all; the bar's Send, away from the box, takes the box's text and files along and says so ("Sent to June with your message"). The agent reads the text first, then the words heard after "Voice note:", and the bubble shows the text above the recording and its words; if it can't be transcribed (a short silent one too, rather than going away), the text waits with it, and Retry or Delete take both. A voice note waiting in its box is kept on the device like any recording not sent, and is back in its box after a relaunch. A photo or file picked or pasted on the device to go with a voice message is kept beside its recording until Claktory has the message, so it still goes after a relaunch or a long wait. One that can't be transcribed says why, with Retry and Delete; one under 5 seconds with no speech in it (sent by mistake, most likely) just goes away, and never reaches the agent. Where the computer can't recognize speech, the mic is greyed out and says why. Settings → Voice shows the speech model (downloading, ready, or why it failed), with Download, Stop, Try again or Remove, and its licence's credit. The first time the mic is used, a small note over it says what it's for ("Talk instead of typing") with **Continue**, which brings the system's question; allowed, the next click or hold records. If it's refused, the box says where to allow it.
- Answering by voice: a field where {{user}} answers an agent (a question's written answer, a review's answer, a checklist reply, the comment on Approve or Dismiss) has a mic at its end, where the Claktory can take voice. Tapped, it records in the field's place (a waveform, the time, Cancel and Done; Esc and Return), and on Done the computer Claktory runs on turns it into text, which goes into the field at the caret, to check and send as typed text; it never sends by itself. The recording goes to no agent; it's kept on the device only until its words come back, then deleted. If the connection drops first, the field says "Not sent yet", with Retry and Delete, and goes by itself once the connection is back; if the app quits first, the recording comes back to its question on the next launch. One whose question has gone (answered on another device, say) is listed in Settings under this device (This Mac, This iPhone…) as **Recordings not sent**, to play or delete. It goes on while {{user}} goes elsewhere in the app, in the same floating bar as a voice message, with what it answers (its name goes back to a question's or a review's conversation), Cancel and Done; Done there with the request still open behind a page or the drawer says where its words go; Done there with its request gone keeps the recording for it and asks for its words at once, and they go into the field when the request is open again. The speech model is asked for, and the mic greyed out, as for voice messages; over 10 minutes is too long.
- Slash commands: typing "/" at the start of the message box lists the agent's commands, filtered as you type; arrows pick one, Enter (or a click) runs it, Tab completes its name to add arguments, Esc closes the list. **/clear** starts a new conversation (the old one is archived; its checklist and memory stay). **/compact** summarizes the conversation now and carries on from the summary; it can't run while the agent is working. Rivet can do either for another agent when you ask it: it compacts one at once (or once its current work ends), and clearing one waits for your approval in a request in Rivet's conversation. If a command is refused, the reason shows above the box. Text starting with "/" that isn't a command is sent as a message.
- {{User}}'s messages are bubbles on the right in the agent's color; the agent's own replies are bubbles on the left, tinted with its color and edged in it, with no name over them (another agent's messages stay filled with that agent's color, under its name). Each message bubble has the time it was sent at its foot ("14:32"), and each of {{user}}'s the glyph of the device it came from; where the day changes, a centered line names the new day (Today, Yesterday, the weekday within the week, else the date). When the agent answers one of {{user}}'s messages with others in between, its reply shows a quote of it on top ("You" and the message's first words); clicking it goes to that message. The conversation grows with the window, up to about twice a comfortable reading width. A long conversation opens at its latest messages, and earlier ones load as {{user}} scrolls up. Scrolled up, a button at the bottom right (on iPhone, Latest at the foot) goes back down to the latest and follows it again, saying how many replies came in meanwhile ("3 new"). It also shows the agent's replies, its thinking (two or more thoughts in a row, with nothing shown between them, are one line, "Thought 5 times", or "Thinking… · 3 so far" while it goes on; click it to see each thought), its tool calls (the calls one reply made share a block; each says on its row what it does, such as the command it runs with "+N lines" when there's more or the file it reads, and at the end how long it has run, ticking (for a command, also when it times out: "1m 23s · times out in 8m 37s", or "no limit" in the background), and once it's done, how long it took; click a call to expand its input and output), compaction dividers, and notices such as errors. An extension's tool calls show what they did with its icon, in a line and the details under it: a message sent ("→" the recipient, then the message, and Sent, or Queued when the recipient was working), the checklist items added or updated with their status and owner, a note read or remembered with its text, a monitor's name and when it fires, a background task's command and whether it still runs, the file shown, a search's results and the page read, a database query's SQL and row count, a secret given to agents or taken back, and the error when one failed. A message, a note or a monitor's prompt shows its first line, with Show more to read it all. The recipient opens their conversation, the file brings its pane forward, and a result or page opens in the browser.
- **Find in a conversation:** ⌘F (Ctrl+F off Apple's) in an agent's or a chat's conversation, or a pipe's (its messages and its liaison's together), opens a find bar at its top right, wherever the keyboard is (the message box too), as do the magnifying glass in the agent's header ("Find") and **Find…** in the conversation's right-click menu; on a phone, **Search** at the top of a ⋯ menu (a conversation's, a chat's, a pipe's, a file's), or the magnifying glass at the top of a page with no other button there (Needs you, a Settings section, a pipe with no liaison), opens it at the foot, above the keyboard and in the message box's place; the keyboard's Search key goes to the next match. Claktory searches the whole conversation since its last /clear, not only what the app has loaded: {{user}}'s messages, the agents' replies, events, and what tool calls were given (a command, a path) and what they printed, not thinking; and the messages still queued at its end (quiet ones too), the newest matches of all. The bar says which match of how many ("3 of 12"; "1000+" past a thousand), Enter or ⌘G goes to the next, up to older ones, ⇧Enter or ⇧⌘G back, loading back as far as the match is; every occurrence is washed yellow, the one gone to in full yellow; a folded tool call or message the match is in opens to show it, and stays open. Esc or **Done** closes it. The same ⌘F (or click there first) finds in what other panes show: a file's viewer (its formatted or plain text, scrolling across a long line), Needs you (in History it goes to History's own search), an agent's Checklist and Memory (a note found opens), its Tasks, Monitors, Messaging and Database panels, and Settings.
- **Copy message**, in the conversation's right-click menu, copies the whole message it's on, as it reads (yours as you typed it), with nothing selected first; while the pointer is over an agent's own reply, a copy button beside its foot does the same, saying **Copied** for a moment. On a phone, touch and hold a message and choose **Copy**.
- **Report…**, in an agent's own reply's right-click menu (on a phone, touch and hold it), reports that reply to Claktory's team, as Google Play asks of apps with AI-generated content. A sheet shows exactly what goes, as Claktory prepared it: the reply's text (only its beginning when it's long), the model that wrote it and this Claktory's version, with a note if {{user}} adds one; nothing else from the conversation, and nothing at all until **Send**. Claktory's team keeps reports 30 days; reporting changes nothing for the agent. A chat's replies have it too; {{user}}'s own messages and other agents' don't.
- An agent's proposal to Rivet shows in its conversation as a request in Rivet's color: the proposal, **Dismiss** and **Approve**, and a field for your words, which turns the buttons into **Submit** once something is typed. Approved, Rivet gets the proposal as your message; submitted with a comment, Rivet gets your comment as what to do, with the proposal for context, and the agent that proposed it is told you answered it so; dismissed, Rivet gets nothing; if it can't (no Rivet, or Rivet has no model), why shows in the request and the proposal waits. Decided, the request says Approved, Answered or Dismissed, when, and your comment.
- When an agent suggests you talk to another agent (`agents_suggest_talk`), its conversation shows why, the message it drafted for you, and **Talk to <name>** in that agent's color. Clicking it opens that agent's conversation with the message in its message box, after anything you'd already typed there, for you to change and send; nothing is sent for you. An agent that's since been deleted shows "<name> was deleted." instead.
- Events, the messages that didn't come from {{user}}, never look like theirs. A message from another agent comes in on the left in a bubble of that agent's color, under its character and name. Click its name to open that agent's conversation; the bubble is for reading, selecting text and following links, like any other. A sender that no longer exists isn't clickable. An agent named with @ (@Kenji) shows as a tag in its color with its character and role; click it to open that agent's conversation. Copied, it's the name. Queued messages from agents work the same. To go back, click the chip at the left of the header ("← Coordinator", naming the agent you came from), press ⌘[ or the mouse's back button; ⌘] or the forward button goes forward again, as in a browser. Agents deleted meanwhile are skipped. Picking an agent or project in the sidebar, Settings or the Marketplace starts over; the way back isn't kept, and each window has its own. Claktory's own notes (such as "Claktory restarted") and finished background tasks are a single centered line; click it to see all of it, such as the task's output.

- Panes: beside its conversation, an agent's main area can show files: text with line numbers, to select and copy (Markdown formatted, with Plain to see its source; a very large file shows its first 2 MB; in formatted Markdown, a picture the file embeds, `![words](shot.png)` by a path beside the file or a full one, shows in place, never wider than the text (PNG, JPEG, WebP, and GIFs, which move), and an MP4 or QuickTime video as a card with Play, so an agent can put a report's screenshots and clips beside it and embed them; a click opens one in its own pane, and a right click or a touch and hold offers Open, Download (on a Mac) and Share…; a picture on the web is never loaded, only a link to open in the browser, a path to any other kind of file is a link that opens it, and one that can't be read shows its words and "Can't show" with its name) and images (fitted to the pane; zoom with − and + in the header, ⌘+ and ⌘− once clicked, ⌘-scroll or a pinch; double-click for 100% and again to fit, or Fit, 100% and ⌘0; on an iPhone, iPad or Android device, or on a slow connection, an image opens as a preview sized to the screen, labelled Preview, and **Show original** loads the full file in its place, as zooming in past the preview's sharpness does by itself) and videos (MP4 and QuickTime: play and pause with the button, a click on the picture or Space, the time, and a timeline to scrub; it's loaded onto the device first, with how far it got; on a slow connection a smaller preview made for the screen comes instead, labelled Preview, with **Show original**). An empty file, an image too big to open (over 20 MB), a video over 2 GB or one that can't be read says so. **Share…** sends the file shown with the device's share sheet (Save Video or Save Image to Photos, Save to Files, AirDrop, Messages, Mail…; Android's share menu): the share button in a viewer's header on a Mac or iPad (on an iPad it stays when a narrow pane hides other controls), first under More on a phone (beside More itself when the file has no comments button). On an iPhone, iPad or Android device, touching and holding a file in a message, in Files or where an agent showed it offers Share… too, and for a message's files **Share N files…** sends them together; on a Mac, right-clicking a message with files offers it. On a Mac, **Download**, beside Share… in a viewer's header, saves the file shown to the Mac's Downloads folder (as "name 2" beside one of the same name already there), with how much has come and Cancel, then says where it went, with **Show in Finder**; the first time, macOS asks whether Claktory may use Downloads. Claktory brings the whole original to the device first, in pieces sized to the connection, with how much has come, Cancel, and Try again picking up where it stopped; a lost connection that doesn't come back within a minute loses at most the last megabyte. Once sent somewhere the copy is kept a day, since the app it went to may read it later (a pasted copy, a mail draft), then deleted; closed or cancelled, it's deleted at once, and anything a share cut short left is deleted at the next launch. A file's name that holds characters which turn text around or show nothing (an agent's file named so an .exe reads as a .pdf) shows them as codes, `\u{202E}`, in pane titles, Files and a message's files. A file opens there when the agent shows it to {{user}}, or when {{user}} clicks it in a project's Files; one already open glows instead. With more than the conversation, each pane has a header with its name: drag a header onto another pane's top, bottom, left or right edge to split that pane equally, drag the line between two panes to resize them (double-click it to share equally again), and close a viewer with its × (the conversation can't be closed). Settings, the Marketplace and What's new open as panes too, right of the conversation, sharing its room, and move, resize and close like any other; they're never narrower than a phone's screen, and scroll sideways when squeezed. The conversation always keeps room to read (about 360 points, more with larger text): when another pane wouldn't fit beside it, the panes opened longest ago close to make room, and in a window too narrow for a page beside the conversation, Settings, Usage and the other pages take the conversation's place instead, with Done (or Esc) to go back. When the window gets narrower after they opened, the conversation keeps its width and the panes beside it are squeezed. Up to 12 panes. **Expand** in a pane's header (every pane but the conversation) fills the whole window with that pane, to read it in peace (comments, selection, scrolling and zoom work there as before); **Back to June** at the top, **Collapse** in its header, or Esc, returns everything exactly as it was. The layout is kept by Claktory, the same on every device, and so is the pane expanded: it stays so after a restart, and a file an agent shows meanwhile opens behind it, waiting, with "June opened notes.md" at the top (a click goes back to it). On iPhone one pane shows at a time: open files are listed first under ⋯, the one most recently opened or shown on top, and one opens as a page over the conversation (a project's Files are under the project instead); Back closes it on the iPhone only, and its × closes the pane everywhere.
- Comments on files: in a text or Markdown viewer, select some text and a popover at the selection offers **Comment**: write the comment right there and press Return (or click Comment) to send it at once to the selected agent (its name on the card; change it from the card), with the quote, so it can revise the file; Shift-Return makes a new line. The agent replies and resolves it with its comments tools. Nothing is set aside for comments while a file has none; once it has some, the text each is on is highlighted in the agent's color and its card sits beside it in a margin at the right, on a pane wide enough to keep the document readable beside it, and otherwise as a small dot in the agent's color at the pane's edge that opens the card at its text. Click a comment or its text to pick it, for Reply, Resolve (resolved ones hide; **Show resolved** in the pane's header brings them back) and, on {{user}}'s own comments, Edit and Delete. Suggest, beside Comment, proposes new text instead (the selection, to edit, and an optional comment); the card shows the old text struck through above the new. On an agent's suggested change, Accept makes it in the file (only while the text is as it was) and Reject leaves the file alone; on {{user}}'s own, Apply makes it and Discard takes it back. Either way the comment is resolved, marked Accepted or Rejected. On an image, a **Comment** button shows at its top right while the pointer is over it (and in the pane's header): then a click marks a spot and a drag an area, and the comment is written beside it. Each comment shows on the image as a numbered pin in its agent's color (an area outlined too), its card has the same number, and a click on one picks it. On a video, the same Comment pauses it at the current frame; then a click or a drag along the timeline marks a moment or a stretch of time, and a click or drag on the frame points at a spot or part of it: each comment shows on the timeline in its agent's color, a click on one plays from there, and its card says when ("0:31–0:45"). A comment on an image or a video stays on its place when the file changes, and its card says it was made on an earlier version.
