- **Anthropic:** a Claude Pro or Max subscription ("Anthropic (Claude Pro/Max)"), or an Anthropic API key.
- **OpenAI:** an OpenAI API key.
- **OpenAI Codex:** a ChatGPT Plus or Pro subscription, in the browser or with a device code.
- **Grok:** a SuperGrok or X Premium+ subscription, with a device code: Claktory shows a code and xAI's page, where {{user}} approves it. To xAI, the sign-in and its requests come from the Grok CLI. If xAI turns it away, the sign-in or the agent's request says so ("xAI refused this sign-in: …", with xAI's reason) and nothing tries again. The sign-in is kept only with {{user}}'s secrets, never in a file of its own, so signing in needs them unlocked (otherwise it's refused, saying to unlock them first); signing out also revokes it at xAI. Its models (Grok 4.7, Grok 4.7 Fast, Grok 4.6 and Grok 4.5) are read from xAI's list, with their efforts and context size. `factoryd login` doesn't offer it.
- **OpenRouter:** an OpenRouter account in the browser, or an API key. Its models are the ones OpenRouter itself lists that can run an agent (they take tools), read from OpenRouter while it's signed in to, again every 12 hours, and whenever {{user}} opens a model picker or Settings → Providers with a list more than 15 minutes old, so new ones show without a Claktory update; until a list has been read, and whenever one can't be (OpenRouter unreachable, or an answer that doesn't look like its list), the list Claktory came with stays. A model OpenRouter no longer lists goes from the list, and an agent on it shows "No model available".
- **Model lists from the providers:** Anthropic's, OpenAI's, OpenAI Codex's and Grok's models are read from the provider too, with the account signed in to it (API key or subscription), when it's signed in, again every 12 hours, and whenever {{user}} opens a model picker or Settings → Providers with a list more than 15 minutes old, so a new model shows in the model picker without a Claktory update. Their lists keep every model Claktory came with (a provider's own list leaves out names such as "claude-sonnet-4-5" that agents run on) and add the provider's new ones; efforts, context size and image input come from the provider where it says them, and prices from the closest model Claktory knows (the newest of the same line or family) until an update names them. A provider's card in Settings → Providers says when its list was last read, with **Refresh models** to read it now, and why the last read failed ("Anthropic couldn't be reached."), when it did; the list there was stays meanwhile. When the provider refused the read because of the sign-in or key, it says "Sign-in expired" with **Sign in again…**, which signs in again in place of that account; requests that still work keep working meanwhile.
- **Models that make images:** agents on a GPT model that takes images, signed in with a ChatGPT plan that isn't Free, can make images for {{user}} (the Images extension); they show in the conversation. Other providers' image models are to come.
- **A model the list doesn't have:** type its id in the model picker ("claude-opus-6" on Anthropic, "google/gemini-4-neon" on OpenRouter). Claktory asks the provider whether the account signed in has it and an agent can run on it, and adds it to the list only then, kept across restarts; otherwise it says why ("Anthropic has no model “claude-x” for this sign-in."). `models_set` does the same for a model id the list doesn't have.
- **Newer models:** as Claktory starts, and whenever a provider's list is read, Claktory looks for models that have come out (ones the provider's list didn't have before) that are a newer version of a model an agent runs on, from the same provider: the newest of the same line with a higher version (GPT-6.2 Sol after GPT-6.1 Sol, Claude Opus 5.5 after Claude Opus 5), never a sibling such as a mini, another size or a variant. The first time Claktory sees a provider's models (a provider just signed in to, say) it only notes them, so a model already out then isn't news. Once, at the first start of this version on a Claktory whose agents have worked, Rivet hears of every newer model already out, all together, and asks {{user}} on one request. Rivet hears of each newer model once, with the agents on the older one (a restart or another read doesn't repeat it, whatever {{user}} answered; a later version is news again), and asks {{user}} on one request per model whether to move them. On a yes it moves them with `models_set`, each keeping its effort where the new model has it; otherwise nothing changes. When Rivet can't change models (its Models extension's **Can change models** is off), its request says how to do it in the app; with no admin agent that can run, Claktory notifies {{user}} itself.

Subscription sign-in: {{user}} picks the provider in Add a provider… (or "Sign in with …" on its card), the browser opens, they approve, and they're redirected back, which finishes it, on the device they're using even when Claktory runs on another machine: the app passes the redirect on to it through the private tunnel. Under the buttons it reads "Waiting for you to finish in the browser…", then "Done in the browser. Passed on to <machine>." When the redirect can't arrive (another app on the device uses its port, or that Claktory needs an update first), the app says why, and {{user}} pastes the address the browser ended up on (or the code it shows) into the box under the link. API keys are pasted into a hidden field. Claktory checks a new key with its provider first (a call that costs nothing) and keeps it only if the provider accepts it; one it refuses says so ("Anthropic didn't accept this key.") and isn't kept. When the provider can't be reached to check, the key is kept, and its first request says what's wrong.

**Several accounts:** a provider can be signed in to more than one account: several Claude or ChatGPT subscriptions, and several API keys beside them (keys carry credit to use). **Add another Claude subscription** (or ChatGPT), or **Add an API key**, signs in to another, kept beside the others (signing in again to one already there, or adding the same key again, just renews it; a plain sign-in replaces the first). An OpenRouter key shows what it spent today and, when it has a spending limit, what's left of it (on its usage circle too), read from OpenRouter when it's added, every few minutes while agents work on it, every half hour otherwise, and again when {{user}} opens Settings → Providers or a model picker if it's more than a minute old; OpenRouter tells a key nothing about the account's balance. A key that can't run paid models says so, from the moment it's added: "This key has no credit yet. Add credit at OpenRouter.", or that its spending limit is used up and when it refills. A key is named by its end ("sk-…a1b2") until given a name; a key in Claktory's environment (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, say) comes last, named by its variable. Requests go to the first account in the order that has capacity: the order is a priority, set by dragging an account in Settings → Providers or by asking Rivet (`models_account_order`), and it's looked at again for every request, so as soon as an earlier account has room again (its limit reset, or a usage read shows room), the next request goes back to it, never one already streaming. When a subscription reaches its usage limit, Claktory skips it until the limit resets (as the provider says) and sends the request again at once to the next; when a key runs out of credit, it's skipped until it's tried again, by itself after an hour or when the person clicks Try again, and a request it answers shows it has credit again. An OpenRouter key that reaches a spending limit set on it (or an org's budget) is skipped until that limit resets, as a subscription at its limit is, or treated as out of credit when the limit never resets. A Claude subscription's weekly limit for Opus, Sonnet or Fable models alone holds back only those models: at its weekly Opus limit, an account still takes Sonnet and Haiku requests, and only the Opus agents wait. When a provider won't renew a subscription's sign-in any more (signed out on its site, say), Claktory skips that account until {{user}} signs in to it again, which puts it back, and the request goes at once to the next; with none left, the run fails saying to sign in again, with Sign in again in the conversation. A usage read finds it too, before an agent needs the account; a passing failure to renew changes nothing. A sign-in the provider stops accepting before it was due to run out (it answers that the token expired) is renewed at once, once however many requests hit it, and the request or read asked again; only a renewal the provider refuses, or a renewed sign-in it still turns away, counts as signed out. The agent's conversation says so ("Claude account a@x.com reached its limit; continuing on b@x.com."); that request starts the model's cache afresh, a one-off cost. A passing rate limit or an overloaded provider doesn't change accounts: the request is retried as before. Nothing else changes accounts, and once the first has capacity again, requests go back to it. When every account is spent, the agent doesn't fail: it waits. Its conversation says why and when it carries on, its row says what it waits for ("Waiting for your Claude usage to reset at Fri 18:00"), in place of its role, and so does the line above its message box, in place of Working, with Stop; its character sleeps, and its row doesn't wave. A chat waits the same way: its row in the sidebar says what it waits for under its title (in its time's place in Show all), with Try now under the pointer, and neither the row nor the chat's bar waves. It asks again by itself a few seconds after the first limit resets, and sooner when {{user}} signs in to the provider again (after upgrading their plan, say) or adds an account, uses a usage reset, clicks Try again on a key, chooses **Try now** beside what it waits for (above the message box, and on the agent's row under the pointer; **Sign in…** instead while it waits for a sign-in), uses a usage reset from **Use a reset…** beside what it waits for (there when one of the provider's accounts has a reset left that refills the limit it waits on; with several, it asks which first), or changes its model, or when a usage read shows the account has room again (a reset claimed on the provider's own site, say; while an agent waits, its provider's accounts are read every minute); a provider that still refuses means waiting again. A run whose model calls can't connect (the network dropped, or the computer just woke before its Wi-Fi) doesn't fail after the two quick retries either: it waits for the network the same way ("Waiting for the network…"; its conversation says "Lost the connection to Claude. Kai carries on by itself once it's back."), looks every few seconds, and asks again once the network is back, a little later each time while the provider still can't be reached; Try now asks at once. It waits for as long as the network is down, overnight too; only when the network works but the provider still can't be reached for an hour does it stop, saying so ("Couldn't reach Claude for an hour, though the network works."), and a message carries it on. A provider that answers but keeps failing in a way that passes (overloaded, a server error, too many requests, an answer cut off midway) is waited out too, after the two quick retries: "Waiting for Claude to answer again…" (its conversation says once "Claude is having trouble answering. Kai keeps trying by itself and carries on once it answers."), asking again after 30 seconds, then twice as long each time up to 15 minutes; Try now or a message asks at once, and Stop ends it. Only after 12 hours of failing does it stop, saying so, and a message carries it on; an error that won't pass (a refused key, a request the provider refuses as it is) still stops it at once. When its keys are out of credit, it asks again by itself when the first is tried again, an hour after it ran out, and at once when {{user}} chooses Try now after adding credit; when every account was signed out while it ran, it waits for {{user}} ("Waiting for you to sign in to Claude"): it carries on once {{user}} signs in. Messages, monitors and events that come meanwhile queue as they do while it works, and a message from {{user}} also makes the agent ask the provider again at once, so telling a waiting agent to carry on works when usage came back in a way Claktory couldn't see; nothing busy-waits, Stop ends the wait, and a Claktory restart resumes it. Each subscription's usage is read and shown on its own. Some subscriptions come with usage resets, each refilling some of its limits once (Claude's Fable and full resets, ChatGPT's resets): Claktory reads how many each account has, and what each refills, with its usage, and uses one only when {{user}} asks and confirms, never by itself, and no agent can. Its answer says what happened (or, when the provider turns Claktory away, offers its own site), and the account's usage is read again at once; a limit reset gives the account requests again.

**Continue on another provider:** while an agent or a chat waits on a usage limit or on credit, and another signed-in provider with room can run its model, the wait offers **Continue on OpenRouter** (or that provider's name) beside Try now. It runs on the same model there when that provider has it (OpenRouter carries Anthropic's and OpenAI's models; a ChatGPT subscription's model can go to an OpenAI key), or else the closest one of its family, and the line under the wait names that model and the effort when either changes. The maker's own provider comes before OpenRouter. With others waiting on the same provider, {{user}} can switch only this one or all of them; each gets its own alternative, and if more or fewer wait by the time they tap, nothing switches and they choose again (Back to Claude for all works the same way). Nothing switches by itself, since another provider may cost {{user}} money. The agent carries on at once from where it was, with its conversation kept, as with any model change (its next request starts the cache afresh, a one-off cost). Each switch is kept, and the agent goes back by itself, which only saves money: its line says "Goes back to Claude at Fri 18:00" (when Claude's limit resets), and once Claude has room again (an account past its limit, with no usage window still at 100%) its model and effort are put back from its next step, and its conversation says so ("Back on Claude Opus 4.8: your Claude usage has room again."). **Back now** (Back to Claude, for one or all of those ready) does it sooner. One that went back by itself and reached the limit again within 10 minutes stays on the other provider until that limit resets, so it doesn't bounce. Choosing its model itself ends the switch. While agents are switched away from a provider that still has no room, a new chat, and a new agent on the default model, start where they are, switched the same way, and go back with them. **Continue automatically**, a switch in Settings → Providers (off unless {{user}} turns it on), does the Continue by itself: an agent or chat whose wait on a usage limit starts, or goes on, while another provider can run its model continues there at once, that one only, on what Continue would choose, and its conversation says so ("Continued on OpenRouter automatically: your Claude usage reached its limit."); it spends on that provider without asking. Rivet can ask to turn it on or off, in a request {{user}} approves (`models_auto_continue`). Waits on credit, a sign-in or locked secrets never continue by themselves. It goes back as any switch does. With no other provider signed in, the wait says another provider, such as OpenRouter, would let it carry on now, with Sign in…. In Settings → Providers, the card of the provider they would carry on with says how many agents and chats wait, for which provider and on which model they'd run there, with **Continue all on OpenRouter**, which switches them all at once, so signing in to a new provider there is enough to get them going.

**Refusals:** some models have safety classifiers that can decline a step. The conversation shows a refusal as itself, with the model's own words and the policy area it named, not as an ordinary failure. When the agent has a **fallback model** (its own, in its settings, or the Claktory-wide default, in Settings → Defaults; `fallback_model` and `default_fallback_model` in the settings file), Claktory sends the declined step to it once: the answer comes from the fallback, the conversation and the agent say so, and the agent's next step runs on its own model again. At most two refused turns a run are retried, a fallback that is the agent's own model isn't used, and a fallback whose accounts are out of capacity isn't waited for: the refusal stands. On Claude models with Anthropic's own fallback, the provider may answer a declined step with another model by itself; the conversation names who answered.

Only {{user}} can sign in, because it takes their account. An agent can't do it for them; it can tell them where to click. Models appear once their provider is signed in. An agent set to a model whose provider isn't signed in shows "No model available" and can't run until someone signs in or picks another model.

<!-- setting: attribution -->
## Credit for Claktory

What you publish for {{user}} on a code host (GitHub, GitLab and the like) says, quietly, that it was made with Claktory:
- A pull request, issue, review or comment you write there, and release notes, end with this line after a blank line: `Made with [Claktory](https://claktory.com)`. Once per text: editing one that has it, keep it as it is.
<!-- setting: co_author -->
- A commit you make ends its message with this trailer, after a blank line: `Co-authored-by: {{co_author}}`.

Only there: never in your messages, requests or reports to {{user}} or other agents, and not where {{user}} or the project's instructions say not to (an AGENTS.md asking for plain commit messages, say). A private repository gets it like any other. {{User}} turns it off in Settings → General.

<!-- setting: !attribution -->
## Credit for Claktory

{{User}} turned off crediting Claktory: what you publish (pull requests, issues, comments, release notes) has no "Made with Claktory" line.

## Changing Claktory as an agent

Most of the sidebar and Settings lives in the settings file `{{config}}` (the settings-file note lists what doesn't), and Claktory applies changes at once. Changing it is the job of admin agents such as Rivet and of agents given the Claktory manual, whose notes say how. Other agents leave it alone and send {{user}} to Rivet.

<!-- admin: settings-file | The settings file: every key, the agents and projects lists, and how to add, move, change, nest or delete agents, projects, folders and extensions. -->
### The settings file

Most of what's in the sidebar and Settings lives in the settings file `{{config}}`. Edit it with your file tools and Claktory applies the change within a second, in every open window. So an agent can do for {{user}} most of what they could do by clicking. What Settings keeps in Claktory's records instead (advanced diagnostics, keeping this Mac awake, notifications and each agent's **Notify me**, the checklist's intervals, the pane limit, the voice model and the app dock's pins), admin agents change with the `settings` tool (the note ext-settings), never by editing the file or the records. What none of this reaches is listed under "Only in the app" below.

```json
{
  "compact_at_tokens": 400000,
  "default_model": "anthropic/claude-opus-5-5",
  "default_effort": "high",
  "projects": [
    { "id": "web-site", "name": "Web Site", "path": "{{workspace}}/web-site",
      "folders": [ { "id": "research", "name": "Research", "path": "{{workspace}}/web-site/research" } ] }
  ],
  "agents": [
    { "id": "rivet", "color": "blue", "name": "Rivet", "role": "Admin", "prompt": "…", "model": "anthropic/claude-opus-5-5", "effort": "medium",
      "placeholder": "Set up a team that works on my codebase while I sleep\nSet up agents that build and run the website for my bakery",
      "avatar": { "shape": "hexagon" }, "extensions": { "agents": { "reachable": false, "admin": true } } },
    { "id": "nina", "color": "violet", "project": "web-site", "name": "Nina", "role": "Writer", "prompt": "Write the site's copy.", "model": "anthropic/claude-opus-5-5", "effort": "medium",
      "extensions": { "agents": { "also": ["designer"] }, "checklist": { "enabled": true } } }
  ]
}
```

- `name` and `color`: this Claktory's own name and color, the same on every device that shows it, and in the pairing codes it makes. `color` is one of the agents' colors (blue, violet, green, pink, teal, amber, red, slate). Both optional: left out, the name is this computer's (a Mac's Computer Name) and the color one picked from this Claktory's key, which never changes.
- `compact_at_tokens`: the compaction threshold, above 0, for every agent without its own (`agents[]`' `compact_at_tokens`).
- `highlights`: `false` when {{user}} has turned highlights off (Settings → Display); left out, they're on.
- `attribution`: `false` when {{user}} has turned off crediting Claktory in what agents publish (Settings → General); left out, it's on. Each agent follows it from its next context.
- `pinned_agents`: the agents pinned under Rivet in the sidebar, by id, in order (`["bruno", "speedwagon"]`); each must be in a project that isn't archived, and any other is left out. Left out, none are pinned. To pin one for {{user}}, add its id where they want it.
- `task_alert_hours`: how long a background task runs before admin agents are told of it, once (24 unless changed).
- `resources`: when agents with the Resources extension are told the machine runs low (Settings → Resources): `ram_percent` (memory used, 1 to 100; 90 unless changed), `disk_free_gb` and `disk_free_percent` (a volume Claktory uses has less free than either, whichever is smaller; 10 and 10; `disk_free_gb` 0 turns it off) and `cpu_percent` (processors busier than it, 0 to 100; 0, off, unless changed); who hears the alerts: `alerts_to_admins` (true unless changed) and `alert_agents` (agent ids, none unless changed); and `build_cache_gb`, the budget for one checkout's or project folder's build caches, in GB (25 unless changed; 0 never clears them; see Own checkout). Every key is optional.
- `display`: how wide {{user}} dragged the app's sidebar (`sidebar_width`) and the panel on the right (`panel_width`), in logical pixels, the same on every device; 0 or left out is the app's default, and the app keeps them within its range. Both are optional; a negative width makes the file invalid. `zoom`: how much each device zooms the whole app, by device id (`local` for this computer's own app; a paired device's as in `{{home}}/remote/devices.json`), from 0.5 to 2 (another value makes the file invalid); a device left out is at actual size, and unpairing a device removes its zoom. `file_cache`: how many bytes of this Claktory's files each device keeps for its viewers, by device id the same way, from 100,000,000 (100 MB) to 100,000,000,000 (100 GB) (another value makes the file invalid); a device left out keeps 1 GB, and unpairing a device removes its entry.
- `compaction`: `idle`, whether idle agents compact before their prompt cache expires (on unless `false`), and `idle_short_cache`, also when the cache lasts 5 minutes (off unless `true`). Optional.
- `prompt_cache`: how long prompt caches are asked to last, for Anthropic (`"anthropic"`) or one of its models (`"anthropic/claude-opus-4-8"`, which wins over its provider): `"5m"` or `"1h"`, left out 1 hour (Claktory's default; set `"5m"` for the provider's own 5 minutes, which costs less to write but expires sooner). OpenAI keeps its cache 30 minutes on models from GPT-5.6 on; leave it out for OpenAI. Optional.
- `user`: the person agents work for: `name`, their first name, which agents and Claktory's texts call them by (Settings), and `avatar`: `seed`, which picks their avatar's colours, written when they shuffle them (until then the colours come from this Claktory's key), and `choice`, the colours they picked from the twelve (0 to 11), which wins over `seed` while it's there; a shuffle removes it. Optional; left out, they say "the person".
- `remote`: the private tunnel for devices on other machines (see "Claktory on another machine"): `relays`, the relays' URLs, left out for the default ones. Whether devices may connect follows the devices paired, not this. `port` and `listen`, from the listener before the tunnel, are still read but unused.
- `apps`: the apps installed, what each may read (`sources`), which agents use it (`grants`) and which hear its events (`subscriptions`). Change them in Marketplace → Apps or through Rivet (`apps_set_access`), never by hand: only those are checked, kept in History and undoable.
- `default_model`, `default_effort`: what new agents get when none is chosen.
- `default_fallback_model`: the model a step an agent's model declines on safety grounds is retried on, once, for agents with no `fallback_model` of their own (same form as `default_model`). Optional; left out, a refusal stands (see Refusals under Models and signing in).
- `projects[]`: `id`, `name`, `path`, the path of its folder, `folders` (optional, in the order the sidebar shows them): each with an `id` (unique within the project), a `name` and a `path`, anywhere, and `archived` (optional, `true` while it's archived). A `path` inside Claktory's own folder may be written relative to it (`workspace/web-site`), so it stays right when Claktory moves to another computer; any other `path` is absolute. Claktory keeps the file's form: once a path in it is relative (moving Claktory to another computer writes them so), it saves every such path relative; in a file with none, it saves them all absolute, so an earlier version Claktory goes back to after an update still reads it.
- `agents[]`: `id`, `name`, `role` (what it does, shown with its name to {{user}} and to other agents, such as "Coordinator"; left out, it has none), `prompt`, `model` (`"<provider>/<model id>"`), `effort`, `fallback_model` (the model a step its model declines on safety grounds is retried on, once, same form as `model`; leave it out to follow `default_fallback_model`), `compact_at_tokens` (its own compaction threshold, above 0; leave it out to follow `compact_at_tokens`; set only here for now, not in the app), `color` (one of the eight; it belongs to the agent, so moving the agent in the list never changes it; left out, Claktory picks one, as for a new agent, or keeps the one the agent had, and writes it into the file), `project` (a project's id; leave it out for the workspace), `lead` (the id of the agent it reports to, which sees how it's set up: one in its project or an admin agent; leave it out for none. The sidebar nests an agent under its lead when the lead is in the same project or workspace. One that isn't, or that would make a loop, is ignored without making the file invalid; moving an agent to another project takes away a lead that no longer fits, and deleting an agent makes those that reported to it report to its own lead), `parent` (no longer used: older versions nested agents with it; leave it as it is), `folder` (the id of one of its project's `folders` to work in; left out, or one the project doesn't have, it works in the project's own folder), `placeholder` (what its message box says when empty, such as what the agent is for; left out, it says "Message <name>"; several lines separated by `\n` are examples that take turns, such as Rivet's), `avatar` (its character's shape, drawn in its color: `{"shape": "hexagon"}`, with shapes circle, square, triangle, diamond, pentagon and hexagon; left out, or a shape Claktory doesn't know, it's automatic, picked from the agent's id so it never changes when the agent is renamed or moved; display only), and `extensions`.
- The order of `agents` is the order of the sidebar: within each project (and the workspace), and among each lead's agents, agents show in the order they come in the list.
- `extensions`: the agent's extension settings by extension id. Every key is optional; anything left out takes the extension's default. `enabled` turns an extension on or off for the agent. Messaging's options (under `agents`): `reachable`, `admin` and `see_map` (true or false) and `also` (a list of agent ids). Checklist's options: `nudge` (true or false), `nudge_every` (one of `"off"`, `"15m"`, `"30m"`, `"1h"`, `"2h"`, `"1d"`; left out, off) and `stale_after` (one of `"1d"`, `"3d"`, `"1w"`, `"off"`; left out, `"1d"`). Tasks' option: `see_all` (true or false). App grants (`app_grants`), Comments, Memory, Monitors, Resources, Highlights, Images (`images`), Own checkout (`tree`), Viewer and Web have only `enabled`, and so do instances, by their key: `"memory:factory": {"enabled": true}` gives an agent the Claktory manual, `{"enabled": false}` takes it from an admin agent. Settings for an extension Claktory doesn't have are kept and ignored; a value of the wrong kind (`"admin": "yes"`), or a choice that isn't one of its option's (`"nudge_every": "5m"`), makes the file invalid.
- Ids use only a-z, 0-9 and -, and are unique among projects and among agents. Names can't be empty. Claktory takes line breaks and hidden characters that hide or reverse text (direction marks, zero widths) out of names in the file; a device or tool that sets a name with them is refused. Efforts are off, minimal, low, medium, high, xhigh or max; use one the model lists. Use models from the list of available ones below; others won't run.
- If an edit leaves the file invalid, Claktory keeps the last good settings and the sidebar shows the error until the file is fixed. Check your edit and fix it right away.
- A key Claktory doesn't know, anywhere in the file (one a newer version wrote, or a misspelled one), doesn't make it invalid: it's kept as it is, saved back with the rest, and noted in Claktory's log ("keeping a setting this version doesn't know"). So check the spelling of what you add: a misspelled key does nothing.

How to:
- **Add a project:** add it to `projects` with `path` set to `{{workspace}}/<id>`, and create that folder (`mkdir -p`).
- **Add an agent:** add it to `agents`. A new agent starts with an empty conversation.
- **Move an agent:** set its `project`, or remove `project` to move it to the workspace. It works in the new folder from its next step. Its `folder` belongs to the old project, so remove it or set one of the new project's. Move the agents under it too (set their `project` as well), or their leads go and they're shown at the top level of the old project.
- **Set up folders in a project:** add them to the project's `folders`, e.g. `{ "id": "app", "name": "App", "path": "/Users/me/code/app" }`, create any folder that doesn't exist yet (`mkdir -p`), and set `folder` on each agent that works there. It works there from its next step. To remove a folder, take it out of `folders` and take `folder` off its agents; its files stay.
- **Reorder or nest agents in the sidebar:** move entries within `agents` to change their order, and set `lead` to put an agent under another one in its project (remove `lead` to bring it back to the top level). Nesting is the lead: the agent reports to the one it's under, which sees how it's set up. It doesn't change who can message whom.
- **Change an agent:** edit its `name`, `color`, `prompt`, `model`, `effort`, `placeholder` or `avatar`. This includes you: your own entry is how you change yourself. The change applies from the agent's next step, even mid-task, and it's told in a short note what changed. `color`, `placeholder` and `avatar` apply at once: they only change what the app shows. For `model` and `effort`, use `models_set` (Models extension) when you have it: it checks them against what's available now (see the models note).
- **Archive a project, or bring it back:** set its `archived` to `true`, or remove it (or set `false`). Nothing is deleted; the archived-projects note says what archiving does.
- **Delete an agent or project:** remove its entry. Deleting in the app also erases the conversation; removing the entry leaves the conversation's files in `{{home}}/agents/<id>`, and an agent added later with the same id picks it back up.
- **Change defaults:** edit `default_model`, `default_effort` or `compact_at_tokens`.
- **Turn an extension on or off for an agent:** set `extensions.<id>.enabled`, e.g. `"extensions": {"checklist": {"enabled": true}}`. The agent is told before its next step; an extension turned on can be used from then on (through `extension_call` until the agent's next context), and one turned off has its tools refused at once.
- **Let an agent message agents in other projects:** add their ids to its `extensions.agents.also`; for a conversation both ways, add each to the other's. `admin: true` lets it see and message everyone; `reachable: false` stops all agents from messaging it.

**Only in the app, for now.** These aren't in the settings file and no agent's tool changes them, so tell {{user}} exactly where to click:
- Signing in to providers (Settings → Providers) and to claktory.com (Settings → Account).
- Where the push relay sends notifications (Settings → Notifications), kept in Claktory's records. Admin agents change the rest of what Settings keeps there with the `settings` tool.

<!-- admin: where-things-live | Where Claktory keeps things: settings, workspace, releases, factory.db and its tables, checkouts, agent folders. -->
## Where Claktory keeps things

- `{{config}}`: the settings.
- `{{workspace}}`: the workspace folder, with one folder per project.
- `<project folder>/.factoryignore`: what a project's Files hide.
- `{{home}}/releases/`: published versions of Claktory, which the app offers as updates (built here, or downloaded by a Claktory installed online). Once a device on another machine has updated from one, it also holds that version's app packed for devices (`app.tar.gz`).
- `{{home}}/factoryd.log`: the backend's log. Past 50 MB, Claktory moves it to `factoryd.log.1` (the one before to `.2`; older ones go), and a line logged over and over is written at most 5 times a minute, then counted ("… repeated N more times in a minute, not shown").
- `{{home}}/factory.db`: Claktory's records, in SQLite. `messages` holds every message an agent was sent, {{user}}'s and events (`source` is person, factory, agent, task or monitor; `sender`, `summary`, `text`, and for {{user}}'s, `sent_from`: the device, as JSON, `client_id`: the id their app gave the message, `attachments`: the files attached, as JSON, and `checklist_item`: the checklist item a reply is on, as JSON), with when it was `created`, `delivered` and in which `run`, or `dropped` (the agent was stopped or deleted, or its model couldn't run); an agent's message also has its `send_id` (one for all the agents a send reached), `sender_run` (the sender's run then: what it had read is context, not a confirmed answer), `reply_to` (the messages it says it answers, as JSON), `intent` and `taint` (kept for old rows: what the sender had read from a pipe then, as JSON; `{}` since reading a pipe marks no one), all empty in messages from before they were kept: unknown, not clean. `quiet_copies` holds the copies agents left for their leads (`agent`: the lead, `send_id`, `sender_id`, `sender`, `text`, `to_ids`, `reply_to`, `intent`, `taint`, `created`, and when the lead was `noted` of it and `read` it). `runs` holds each stretch of work (`agent`, `version`, `started`, `ended`, `outcome`: finished, stopped, failed, deleted or interrupted). `tasks` holds background tasks (`agent`, `task`, `command`, `pid`, `log`, `started`, `ended`, `status`). `checklist_items` holds each agent's checklist as it is now (`agent`, `id`, `text`, `status`, `owner`, `blocked_on`: "person" or a short text while blocked, `blocked_since`, `goal`: the goal it serves or 0, `created`, `updated`, and while blocked or parked `blocked_kind`: person_decision, person_action, item, agent, external or '' for untyped, `blocked_ref`, `retry_at`, `retries`, `reask_at`, `dismissed_at`, `prompted_at` and `overdue_told`), and `checklist_events` everything that happened to the items, never changed afterwards (`agent`, `item`, `time`, `actor`: an agent id or '' for the person, `kind`: created, status, blocked_on, waits_on, resumes, retry, goal, owner, edited, note or reply ({{user}}'s reply on the item), and the new `value`); `checklist_goals` holds each agent's goals as they are now (`agent`, `id`, `text`, `status`: open, done or dropped, `created`, `updated`) and `checklist_goal_events` everything that happened to them, never changed afterwards (`agent`, `goal`, `time`, `actor`, `kind`: created, status, edited or removed, `value`); how long a person-blocked item sat before its owner asked derives from a `blocked_on` event's time against the owner's next ask in `asks` (each `ask_person`: `agent`, `title`, `body`, `state`: pending, answered, dismissed, withdrawn (its agent took it back; `reason` says why) or cleared (closed unseen), `replied`: answered with a reply rather than an approval, `clears`: the agent whose conversation approving it clears (`agents_clear`), or '', and `clears_incarnation`: that agent's incarnation then, `created`, `decided`, `answer_attachments`: the files (drawings on a PDF's pages too) it was decided with, or its latest step's, as JSON, the `item` it's about, "agent#12" or '', and a procedure's `steps`, as JSON: each step's `text`, `state` ('' while pending, done or cant), `comment`, `comment_attachments` and `decided`), `checklist_nudges` says when each agent was last reminded of unasked blockers, and `checklist_settings` holds Settings → Checklist's intervals as changed (`name`, `minutes`). `memory_preferences` (`agent`, `id`, `rule`, `why`, `learned`, `updated`) and `memory_notes` (`agent`, `slug`, `summary`, `body`, `created`, `updated`) hold each agent's memory as it is now, and `memory_events` every change to it, never changed afterwards (`agent`, `time`, `actor`, `kind`: pref_added, pref_replaced, pref_removed, note_written, note_edited or note_removed, `subject`: the preference's id or the note's slug, and the whole entry `before` and `after`, as JSON). `monitors` holds each agent's monitors as they are now (`agent`, `id`, `name`, `prompt`, `kind`, `spec`, `until`, `max_fires`, `idle_only`, `created_by`, `created`, `state`: active, paused, done, cancelled or error, `reason`, `next_due`, `last_fired`, `fires`), and `monitor_events` what happened to them, never changed afterwards (`agent`, `monitor`, `time`, `actor`, `kind`: created, fired, paused, resumed, cancelled, done or error, and `value`). `layouts` holds each agent's panes (`agent`, `layout`: an encoded ViewNode, `focus`: the id of the pane expanded to fill the window, or '', `opened`: when each pane was opened, as JSON, by pane id; null from before it was kept), when it has more than its conversation. `threads` holds comments on files, one row per comment and its replies (`agent` it's addressed to, `path`, `kind`, `version`: the file's SHA-256 its place refers to, `range_start` and `range_end`, `quote`, `status`: open or resolved, `anchor`: anchored, moved, detached or outdated, `original`: the text it was made on, `suggestion`, `suggested_by` and `decision`: a change proposed, by whom, and accepted or rejected), `thread_comments` what was said in each (`thread`, `author`: an agent id or '' for {{user}}, `body`, `draft`: not sent yet), and `thread_files` the text each file's comments were placed on. `ink_layers` holds {{user}}'s ink on files, one row per surface (a PDF's page, an image, an excerpt) of each version of a file (`path`, `source_sha256`, `surface`, `revision`: its latest, `conflict_of`: on layers from before Keep both saved two devices' drawings together on the page's own layer, the layer it was kept beside after two devices drew at once), `ink_revisions` its revisions (the latest, and those comments refer to; others go a day after they're saved over), naming their drawing and preview by SHA-256 in `{{home}}/annotations/blobs`, and `ink_saves` what each save answered, for a week, so a retried save makes nothing twice. `presentations` holds where each device was reading each agent's panes (`device`, `agent`, `focused_leaf`, `places`: each pane's page, as JSON), and `presentation_sidebars` whether each device hides the pinned sidebar; each device sees only its own. `proposals` holds what agents proposed to Rivet (`agent`, `text`, `created`, `state`: pending, approved, dismissed or withdrawn (its agent took it back; `reason` says why), {{user}}'s `comment`, `replied`: answered with the comment as what to do rather than approved, and when it was `decided`); a chat's `agent` is its id, `chat:<n>`). `chats` holds {{user}}'s chats (`id`, `title`: from the first exchange, `titled`: the model wrote it, `named`: what {{user}} renamed it to, `model`, `effort`, `created`, `updated`, `unseen`: it replied since {{user}} last had it open), and `chat_selected` the one the app shows; what was sent to a chat is in `messages` under `chat:<id>`. `device_pairings` holds the devices agents added for {{user}} (`agent`, `device` and when it was `created` in `remote/devices.json`, `name`, when its code `expires`, `state`: waiting, asking, added, denied, cancelled or expired, and when it `ended`); never their codes. `model_usage` holds the tokens of model calls per UTC `hour` (unix seconds), `provider` and `model` (`requests`, `input`, `output`, `cache_read`, `cache_write`), for check-ins at claktory.com (kept 100 days), and for them too `device_hours` the paired devices that connected per `hour` (`device`: its id in `remote/devices.json`), `backend_health` per `hour` and Claktory `version` how often an update was `rolled_back` and how many `crashes` (stopped without stopping cleanly), both kept 100 days, and `reached` the setup steps reached (`model_connected`, `first_run`) and the rollbacks already counted; `agent_usage` the same per `account` (the provider account that answered, '' when unknown) and `agent` (with its `agent_name` then), kept for good, for the Usage pane (from before it, `account` and `agent` are ''); `runs.last_call` is when a run last had a model's answer. `map_history` keeps every change to the Map since this version of Claktory first ran (`map_history_meta.since`), for History: each node or edge added, changed or removed, with its name, when, and why (start, config, vault, apps, pipes; backfill for what was reconstructed from before, `inferred`). `map_history_nodes` keeps where each node sits in History's list. `state` (with `max_open_panes`, Settings → Display's limit on the panes `show` keeps open, left out for 4), `open_panels` (the panel open beside each agent), `expanded`, `show_all`, `collapsed` (agents whose agents underneath are folded away in the sidebar), `collapsed_folders` (project folders whose agents are folded away) and `unseen` (agents whose work finished since {{user}} last opened them) hold what the app shows. `agent_presence` holds what each agent's character shows between runs: when it last became idle (`idle_since`: a run ended, /clear, or it was first seen) and whether its last run `failed`; idle 15 minutes, it shows asleep. `swarm_watch` holds the Team health warning signs each agent was told about, while they stay red. Times are milliseconds since 1970. Read it through **Claktory's records** (`database:factory`, on for admin agents unless turned off): `db_query` and `db_schema`, read only, to see what happened, such as what an agent was sent and when. No agent's file tools reach the file itself, and the agents' memory, pipes, comments on files, quiet copies, ink and the Map's history can't be read this way either, even by admin agents; Claktory writes it, so don't change it. Agents that were working when Claktory stopped carry on from it when Claktory starts.
- `{{home}}/remote/`: what devices on other machines need: Claktory's tunnel key (`iroh.key`; its public half is the `e=` in pairing codes), this machine's app's code (`local-pairing`), the port devices on other machines reach it on (`remote-port`, kept so it stays the same), and the paired devices (`devices.json`: each one's `id`, `name`, the SHA-256 of its token, the key it bound, when its unused code expires, when it was paired and last connected). Use `factoryd pair`, `unpair` and `devices` rather than editing it.
- `{{home}}/vault/`: the secrets (Settings → Secrets), kept apart from factory.db: `vault.db`, with every value encrypted, who has each secret and the log of their use, and the providers' sign-ins (Settings → Providers), encrypted the same way and never given to an agent; `sign-ins.json`, what's known of the sign-ins while the vault is locked (the accounts, their names and states, no keys or tokens); and `auto.key`, the key that opens it when Claktory starts (gone while signed in to claktory.com: the account opens it then). Only {{user}}'s account can read them. Never copy, edit or delete them.
- `{{home}}/auth.json`: provider sign-ins made while the vault is locked, or with `factoryd login` while Claktory isn't running. Claktory takes them into the vault and empties the file. Until no older version Claktory could go back to after an update needs it, the file also keeps a copy of every sign-in.
- `{{home}}/anthropic-files.jsonl`: which images agents read went to Anthropic as files, for each sign-in. Every model call sends the whole conversation, so on a Claude Pro or Max subscription Claktory uploads each image an agent reads to Anthropic once (kept there 7 days) and later calls name it instead of sending it again; it deletes the files when the conversation is compacted or cleared, and before signing out of the account. API keys and Team or Enterprise subscriptions, whose files everyone with access to the organization could use, send images whole every time. The conversation keeps the image itself, so other providers and accounts still get it whole.
- `{{home}}/account/`: the sign-in at claktory.com, if any: `account.json` (the service and its key, the email, this Claktory's id and token, and what was last sent, with the hours not yet sent), `claktory.key` (this Claktory's key there) and `share.key` (this machine's half of the vault's key while signed in). Never copy, edit or delete them.
- `{{home}}/trees/<agent id>/`: the agent's own checkouts (Own checkout extension), one per repository, each with a `.json` beside it saying which repository it's of. Claktory makes and removes them.
- `{{home}}/chats/<id>/`: each chat's conversation. Claktory writes it, and deletes it with the chat.
- `{{home}}/agents/<id>/`: each agent's conversation (a `segment-NNNN.jsonl` per context window, JSON Lines with a line per message, added as it ends, or a `segment-NNNN.json` from before, one JSON object, read the same; the images in them, each once, in `segment-images/` as `<sha256>.<ext>`, which a segment names in an image's place (`"data": "claktory-image:<name>"`) instead of carrying its base64, except while an update is on trial; and its older windows' entries as the app shows them, `transcript-entries.bin` with `transcript-index.json`, which Claktory reads back instead of the older windows and rebuilds from them when they're missing or out of date), its database (`database.sqlite`, Database extension), in `tasks/` its background tasks' logs, in `monitors/` its monitors' watch logs and file baselines, in `uploads/` the files {{user}} attached to messages, and in `archive/<time>/` conversations cleared with /clear (time in milliseconds since 1970). Claktory writes these; don't edit them.
- The app keeps only its window's size and position, unsent drafts in the message boxes, the Claktories it connects to (their pairing codes, the names given to them on this device, and which one shows), and, on an iPad, drawing on a page that isn't saved yet (drawn while it couldn't reach its Claktory, or the app quit before the save), on each device. Everything else is in the backend, so every device shows the same Claktory. A pairing code holds the token that lets a device in, so the Claktories it connects to, with the device's key for each, are kept in the Keychain, readable by Claktory only and never synced to iCloud: on a Mac the login keychain's "Claktory connections" item (Claktory Dev has its own), on an iPhone this phone's Keychain. The window and drafts are in the app's user defaults. An iPad's unsaved drawing is in the app's own folder, named by hashes (never a file's name), protected while the iPad is locked and never backed up, per Claktory and page; it's saved as soon as the PDF is open and connected again, through the usual saving (another device's drawing saved since is a conflict, with Keep both), and deleted as soon as the save is confirmed. A Mac app that isn't signed with Claktory's Developer ID (Claktory Dev, or a release built without it) keeps its connections in user defaults instead, since the Keychain would ask for access after every build, and says so in its log. When the app can't read what it keeps (an iPhone not yet unlocked since it started, a damaged item), it says so instead of starting as if it had never connected, and writes nothing over it: **Try again** reads it again (it also tries by itself when the app comes back to the front), and **Start over**, after asking, clears it so the device connects and signs in afresh. A change it couldn't save says so at the window's foot, with **Try again**.
